FILE: AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt (SHA-256 72cd0e9bb8eb5971717e247cf4e8b7207ed5879513d1a1eb09ddb5ec1044389c)
AMBIGUITY LOG, O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0, tests T3 and T14a, vector profile v0
Package first issued 7 Nov 2026; revision c issued 10 Nov 2026. Source text: DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23857888, Markdown source SHA-256 8758174e927e971391443ce9142f9c78bafad21ad52f63613d2443a422869d3d.
Each entry: where the document is silent or unclear, the question, the readings considered, the reading used, and which vectors change if the reading changes. Every reading used here is a vector profile v0 choice. None is normative.

1. Encoding (C2; "Planned for the next version": one normative wire profile).
Question: which canonical encoding do T3 records use? Readings: deterministic CBOR; JCS (RFC 8785). Used: JSON with JCS rules for a restricted subset (ASCII keys, strings, integers, booleans; no floats, no nulls). For that subset Python's json.dumps with sorted keys, compact separators and ensure_ascii False gives the same bytes as JCS. Effect: every T3 hash, so every T3 vector, changes if CBOR is chosen.

2. Keys are ASCII only (C2).
Question: JCS sorts keys by UTF-16 code units; Python sorts by code points. They differ only for keys outside the Basic Multilingual Plane. Used: profile v0 keys are fixed ASCII names, so the difference never arises. Effect: none on current vectors; a future profile with free-form keys must sort by UTF-16 code units.

3. Number rules (C2; next-version wire profile lists "number and null rules").
Question: which numbers are allowed? Used: integers only, from minus (2^53 minus 1) to plus (2^53 minus 1); counters and seq additionally non-negative. A number written with a fraction or exponent (100.0) is rejected even when its value is whole. Booleans are never integers (Python treats true as 1). Effect: t14a-012, t14a-013, t14a-014 return HOLD; under a looser reading they would PASS or be read differently by readers in other languages.

4. Duplicate keys (C2; next-version wire profile lists duplicate-key rejection).
Question: what happens to an input with a repeated key? Used: reject the whole input with proposed code INPUT-DUPKEY, result FAIL, and walk nothing. FAIL rather than HOLD because a repeated key lets two readers see two different records, which is the attack C2 names. Effect: t3-021. Under a HOLD reading t3-021 returns HOLD.

5. NFC (C2).
Question: which strings must be NFC, and what result? The text says the verifier rejects non-NFC input. Used: every string, keys included; proposed code T2-NFC, FAIL; the T3 walk still runs and its findings are also reported. Effect: t3-018. If NFC is checked only in record data, nothing changes today.

6. Hash input (C3, C2).
Question: what bytes are hashed? Used: SHA-256 over the canonical bytes of the record without its own "hash" field, stored as 64 lowercase hex characters. No domain separation tag (the next version plans one). Uppercase hex is malformed (T3-MALFORMED), since accepting both cases lets two strings stand for one hash. Effect: every T3 vector if a domain tag is added; t3-022.

7. Numbering and genesis (C3).
Question: zero-based or one-based seq; what prev_hash does the first record carry? Used: one-based, first prev_hash is 64 zero characters. Effect: t3-014 (zero-based) FAILs and t3-001 PASSes; they swap if zero-based is chosen. t3-028 depends on the genesis value.

8. Teardown shape (C3, C13).
Question: the text says only that the teardown carries last_seq. Used: the teardown is a record of type "teardown" in the same stream, its seq is last_seq plus 1, it links to the final event, exactly one per stream, nothing after it. Effect: t3-005, t3-009, t3-017, t3-024, t3-025, t3-027.

9. Open stream (C3: "no gaps"; T3 pass criterion).
Question: is a stream with no teardown a PASS? Used: HOLD with proposed T3-OPEN, because tail completeness cannot be shown. Effect: t3-006. Under a PASS reading, tail deletion of open streams would pass silently.

10. Empty input (C3).
Question: what does T3 return with no streams? Used: HOLD with proposed T3-EMPTY. Effect: t3-013.

11. Walk order (C3).
Question: is file order meaningful? Used: no; the verifier walks each stream by seq. Effect: t3-002 and t3-015 PASS; under a file-order reading they would fail.

12. Link rule across a missing seq (C3: "valid links").
Question: when seq n minus 1 is absent, what does record n's prev_hash have to match? Readings: (a) nothing, the gap is reported by T3-GAP alone; (b) the nearest lower record present. Used: (b). Reason: it separates a deletion (seq 4 still points at the deleted record: T3-GAP plus T3-LINK, t3-003) from a declared capture skip (seq 4 links straight to seq 2: DECLARED GAP only, t3-019). Effect: t3-003, t3-005, t3-020 lose T3-LINK under reading (a).

13. Order of checks (C3).
Question: the brief lists checks (a) to (f) in order; does an earlier failure stop later checks? Used: all checks run and every code that fires is printed. A record that is malformed takes its whole stream out of the walk, to avoid noise from a record the verifier cannot read. Effect: t3-022 reports only T3-MALFORMED.

14. Malformed records (C3; the text has no malformed-input rule).
Question: what does T3 do with a missing field, wrong type, unknown field or bad hex? Used: proposed T3-MALFORMED, HOLD. Effect: t3-022, t3-023.

15. Reason codes (no registry yet; "Planned for the next version": a registry of reason codes).
Question: what codes exist? Used: the list in REASON_CODES_PROPOSED.txt, every one marked proposed, not normative. Effect: every expected.txt prints codes; a registry would rename them.

16. Several reasons at once (common output rule).
Question: how are multiple reasons printed and combined? Used: every code once in the summary line, sorted alphabetically, then one detail line per occurrence (a code can fire on several records). Overall result FAIL if any FAIL code, else HOLD if any HOLD code, else PASS. The brief asked for one detail line per code; one line per occurrence was chosen so that no finding is hidden. Effect: t3-007, t3-009, t14a-005 print several lines per code.

17. Declared gaps (V2.3; A1; Known issues: two senses of "gap").
Question: how is a declared gap expressed, and what is the result for its scope? The text says a declared gap is "never PASS" for its scope and an undetected-but-undeclared gap is a FAIL. Used: declared_gaps entries are {stream_id, from_seq, to_seq, action_type, reason}; any declared gap gives proposed code DECLARED-GAP, HOLD, and is listed under "Declared gaps". A missing seq outside the declared range is an UNDECLARED GAP and fails. Effect: t3-019 (HOLD), t3-020 (FAIL).

18. Fail-open drops (A1 against V2.3; Known issues).
Question: A1 says counted drops under a declared fail-open policy "are reported, and do not fail". V2.3 says a declared gap is "never PASS" for its scope. Readings: primary (brief): PASS with a DECLARED GAP line; Reading D: HOLD with DECLARED-GAP. Used: primary, as the brief instructs. Recommendation to the editor: Reading D. It keeps one rule for every declared gap, it matches "never PASS", and A1's "do not fail" is still met because HOLD is not FAIL. Effect: t14a-002 becomes HOLD under Reading D (alt-d-001 shows it).

19. Fail-open drops under a fail-closed declaration (A1: "declared fail-open policy").
Question: if the declaration says fail-closed but a checkpoint says fail-open, are its drops a declared gap? Used: no. The policy was not declared, so the drops are not listed as a declared gap and the mismatch fails with proposed T14A-POLICY. Effect: t14a-016.

20. Counters "balance" (C14, T14a). Three readings.
Primary: received equals accepted plus dropped at every checkpoint. Reading B: also, no counter falls from one checkpoint to the next (proposed T14A-REGRESS). Reading C: also, accepted at a checkpoint equals the number of records in the chain up to that checkpoint (proposed T14A-CHAIN). The words "counters balance" settle none of this. Recommendation to the editor: adopt Reading B into T14a now (it needs nothing beyond the checkpoints, and a counter reset is a cheap way to hide a loss), and make Reading C its own test under C14 tied to T3, because it needs the chain as well as the counters and fails for a different reason. Effect: alt-b-002 and alt-c-002 FAIL only under their readings. No primary T14a vector changes under Reading B, because every primary multi-checkpoint vector has counters that never fall.

21. Counter provenance (C14; Conventions: independent source).
Question: who sets the provenance label, and what does UNVERIFIED do? Used: the label defaults to SOURCE-LOCAL; UNVERIFIED gives proposed T14A-UNVERIFIED, HOLD, because a check resting on an UNVERIFIED copy can FAIL but never PASS; a label of INDEPENDENTLY-RECONCILED written in the operator's own input is printed as "claimed in input, not checked" and changes nothing. Effect: t14a-017, t14a-018.

22. Drops under fail-closed (T14a against T14b).
Question: should T14a fail when dropped is above zero under fail-closed? Used: no; that is T14b's job, which is not run. T14a PASSes with the line "dropped greater than zero under fail-closed: T14b not run". Effect: t14a-005, t14a-007.

23. Malformed checkpoints and duplicates (C14).
Used: missing, non-integer, negative or out-of-range counters, unknown fields, missing policy and a repeated checkpoint_id are proposed T14A-MALFORMED, HOLD; the malformed checkpoint is set aside and the rest are checked. Effect: t14a-009, t14a-010, t14a-012 to t14a-015.

24. Padding versus gap (A1 definition of gap).
Question: is accepted plus dropped above received a gap? Used: it fails T14A-IMBALANCE but is not listed as a gap, because nothing is missing. Effect: t14a-004.

25. Signed counters (C14 says "signed ... counters").
Question: T14a vectors carry no signatures. Used: signature checks belong to T5 and are not run here; T14a is arithmetic only. Effect: none on current vectors.

26. Declaration shape and T1b (A1).
Question: what fields does the declaration carry? Used: consequential_action_types, tolerances {max_gap, max_clock_skew_s, max_unsealed_window_s}, fail_mode, monitors (list of names), declared_gaps. T1b gating (DECL-EMPTY, DECL-LOOSE) is not run; the strength line ends "T1b: not run". Every vector uses the A1 default bounds (max gap 0, skew 5 s, unsealed window 60 s). A declaration outside this shape gives proposed DECL-MALFORMED, HOLD. The tolerance max_gap is printed but not applied (no vector uses a non-zero gap tolerance). Effect: none today; a non-zero max_gap reading would need new vectors.

27. "Covering [n of m] consequential action types" (A1).
Question: how does a gap defined by stream and seq "cover" an action type? Used: each declared gap names one action_type; n is the number of distinct declared action types named by declared gaps. Effect: t3-019, t3-020 print "covering 1 of 1". Under DECL-LOOSE (not run) this declaration would be HOLD because declared gaps cover every action type; that is a sign the field needs a clearer definition.

28. Profile (Three profiles: "the profile is printed on every result").
Used: Profile F, since C3 sequence numbers are assigned at the source only under F (C3 data availability N for W). Profile W vectors (verifier assigns seq on ingestion, and the PASS line "integrity of received records; source completeness not established") are out of scope. Effect: every expected.txt prints "Profile: F".

29. Verdict layout (common output rule; Scope; V2.3; V2.4).
Question: the common output rule fixes six items; Scope and V2.3 add lines without placing them. Used: strength line; clock grade; sampling; test, suite and evidence hash; profile; result and reason codes with details; counters and notes (T14a); declared gaps; undeclared gaps; coverage windows (not run); T14e-SHADOW (not run); disclosure; V2.4 sentence in full (which contains item 5's line); T6c line; five shortened scope lines. Effect: layout of every expected.txt.

30. Disclosure wording (common output rule item 4; C21).
Used: "Verifier disclosure: no fee, volunteer run, no financial relationship with the operator". It is placed after the gap lists rather than on the result line. Effect: every expected.txt.

31. Evidence hash (V2.4).
Used: SHA-256 of the input.json file bytes exactly as stored. A Git checkout that rewrites line endings changes this hash; the package ships .gitattributes ("* -text") for that reason. Effect: every expected.txt.

32. Exit codes and output bytes.
Used: 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 HOLD, 3 usage error; output forced to UTF-8 with LF line endings so that byte comparisons hold on Windows. Effect: expected_exit.txt in every vector folder. Revision b (7 Nov 2026): a file that cannot be read and any internal error now exit 3 with no verdict. Before revision b these exited 1, the FAIL code, so a script could not tell a crash from a FAIL. Revision c (10 Nov 2026): the exit-3 paths are pinned by check_exit_codes.py (they give no verdict, so they cannot be vectors), and deep nesting no longer reaches Python at all (entry 36). No earlier vector output changed.

33. Known limits kept as vectors.
t3-006 (open stream tail), t3-012 (full rewrite from the point of tampering) and t14a-011 (consistent under-reporting) PASS or HOLD where an attack happened. They show what T3 and T14a cannot see alone: C13, C6/T6a and T14c respectively.

34. File names (brief house rule 9 against Part 2 names).
The brief asks for dated file names and also names files README.txt, verify.py and so on. Used: the package folder carries the date (o4m-record-tests-vectors-2026-11-07) and the bundle file is dated; the files inside keep the names Part 2 gives, so that commands in the documents work. Revision b added the letter b to the folder and bundle names. Revision c (10 Nov 2026) is named by its issue date and a letter, o4m-record-tests-vectors-2026-11-10b, because its vector inputs are the 7 Nov inputs plus three added vectors. A first build of revision c, also dated 10 Nov, was superseded the same day by the b build (second-reader packet refreshed) and was never published.

35. Byte order mark (C2; RFC 8259 section 8.1 forbids adding a BOM to JSON sent over a network and lets a parser ignore one).
Question: what happens to a file that starts with a UTF-8 byte order mark, as some Windows editors save? Used: the verifier does not strip it, so the input is INPUT-UNPARSEABLE, HOLD. Effect: vector t3-030 (added in revision c, 10 Nov 2026) pins HOLD with INPUT-UNPARSEABLE. An operator export saved in such an editor will HOLD until the BOM is removed; a future profile should say whether to strip, reject or HOLD.

36. Maximum nesting depth (C2; found by a red-team run of hostile inputs, 7 Nov 2026).
Question: how deeply may a JSON input nest? Before revision c the answer depended on the Python build. 100,000 nested arrays gave HOLD (INPUT-UNPARSEABLE, exit 2), but 5,000 nested objects parsed and then crashed the string walk with no verdict (exit 3), and where the cut-off fell depended on the interpreter's recursion limit. An operator could therefore choose a no-verdict outcome over a HOLD by shaping the file, and two honest verifiers on different Pythons could disagree on the same bytes. Readings considered: leave to the interpreter; a fixed limit. Used: a fixed limit of 16 levels (real inputs reach 4), counted by an iterative byte scan of [ and { outside strings, before parsing. Deeper input is HOLD with INPUT-MALFORMED, "nesting deeper than 16". The string walk is iterative as well. Effect: vectors t3-029 (17 levels, HOLD), t3-031 (exactly 16 levels, passes the scan) and mutants M-IN-06 to M-IN-08. The number 16 is a vector profile v0 choice; a different limit changes only t3-029 and t3-031.
END OF FILE: AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt

FILE: CHANGES_2026-11-07b.txt (SHA-256 ccdafdddb517e08f2561849cbdda54013ac319606dca9c052a4fc0465af9da7e)
CHANGES, package o4m-record-tests-vectors-2026-11-07b (from 2026-11-07), 7 Nov 2026
Every input.json, expected.txt and expected_exit.txt is byte-identical to the 2026-11-07 package. The 52 vectors, the second-reader inputs and every verdict are unchanged.

1. verify.py: a file that cannot be read (missing, a folder) and any internal error now exit 3 with a message and no verdict. Input nested too deeply to parse is HOLD with INPUT-UNPARSEABLE. Before, all of these exited 1, the code for FAIL, so a script could not tell a crash from a FAIL. Found by a red-team run of hostile inputs (100,000 nested brackets, a missing file, a folder). No vector pins this yet; a later revision should add one.
2. README.txt: "Disclaimer and terms of use" added, matching the VerZan Check repository (AS IS, no warranty, not a rating or certificate, no professional relationship, limits of liability, consumer rights, trade marks, English law). Run order fixed: check SHA256SUMS before running mutate.py, which rewrites the header of MUTATION_RECORD.txt with your own run's date and Python version. Mac and Windows commands named. Python versions actually run stated.
The repository address is filled in (github.com/StSForensics/o4m-record-tests); the public comment log address is still to be added.
3. NOTICE: one line on AS IS and not legal advice.
4. REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt added: the vectors indexed by three supervisor questions, with what they cannot show. Reading aid only. Added to the list of files never given to a second reader.
5. AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt: entry 32 records the exit-code change; entry 34 notes the b suffix; entry 35 added (UTF-8 byte order mark gives HOLD).
6. mutation/MUTATION_RECORD.txt: re-run against the changed verify.py. Same result as before: 50 mutants, 49 killed, 1 equivalent mutant survives (M-T3-06).
7. MANIFEST.json, SHA256SUMS and the bundle (now bundle_2026-11-07b.txt) regenerated.
Status unchanged: self-run, generated with AI assistance, not reproduced by anyone outside the editor.
END OF FILE: CHANGES_2026-11-07b.txt

FILE: CHANGES_2026-11-10b.txt (SHA-256 33356d617a9fba1609bc73bbfbf0721c2dadf8ac91a1e944c01c9ecbe1406cb2)
CHANGES, package o4m-record-tests-vectors-2026-11-10b (revision c of the package first issued 7 Nov 2026), 10 Nov 2026. This b build supersedes an earlier build of the same day that was never published; do not use that one.
Every input.json, expected.txt and expected_exit.txt of the 52 vectors in 2026-11-07b is byte-identical here (checked with diff). Three vectors are added, so there are 55 (50 input files and 5 alternative readings).

1. verify.py: maximum nesting depth 16, counted by an iterative byte scan before parsing. Deeper input is HOLD with INPUT-MALFORMED, "nesting deeper than 16". Before, 100,000 nested arrays gave HOLD but 5,000 nested objects crashed the string walk (exit 3, no verdict), and the cut-off depended on the Python version. Both now give the same HOLD on 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13. The string walk is iterative. AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt entry 36.
2. Vectors added: t3-029 (17 levels, HOLD INPUT-MALFORMED), t3-030 (UTF-8 byte order mark, HOLD INPUT-UNPARSEABLE; entry 35 already decided this), t3-031 (exactly 16 levels, passes the depth scan, HOLD T3-MALFORMED).
3. Mutants added: M-IN-06 (depth check removed), M-IN-07 (limit 17), M-IN-08 (limit 15). Mutation record re-run: 53 mutants, 52 killed, 1 equivalent mutant survives (M-T3-06, unchanged and documented). New vectors kill the new mutants; no expected.txt was edited to kill a mutant.
4. check_exit_codes.py added: runs the missing-file, folder, no-argument, unknown-test and bad-reading cases and asserts exit 3 with no verdict line, plus a valid input as a control. Seven cases, all pass on 3.11.18, 3.12.3 and 3.13.18. The unreachable internal-error path is reviewed by reading only.
5. gen_vectors.py: an OUTDIR that starts with "-" is refused with the usage line and exit 3. Before, "python gen_vectors.py --expected" wrote 52 vectors into a folder called "--expected".
6. README.txt: counts updated; run line for check_exit_codes.py; Python versions now stated from recorded runs (3.11.18, 3.12.3, 3.13.18, Linux), with the result lines in MANIFEST.json; the sentence "StS Forensics Ltd has not yet started trading" removed from the disclaimer (the VerZan page no longer carries it). The Patents paragraph is unchanged and still needs the editor's decision.
7. REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt: three lines added. NOTICE unchanged. Copyright holder unchanged: Gareth Wong, in every file.
8. Package named by its issue date and a letter (2026-11-10b). CHANGES_2026-11-07b.txt is kept for history. MANIFEST.json, SHA256SUMS and the bundle (bundle_2026-11-10b.txt) regenerated.
9. SECOND_READER_PACKET refreshed: INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_SECOND_READER_2026-11-10.txt (version 2) replaces the 7 Nov file. It adds a three-stage handover, a note that some inputs are hostile on purpose, and a rule not to search the web for the tests. pass2_inputs now holds 50 files: the 47 earlier inputs plus t3-029, t3-030 and t3-031. The depth limit and the byte order mark are profile choices that the specification does not state, so a blind reader is expected to differ on those three; that is informative, not a fault.
Status unchanged: self-run, generated with AI assistance, not reproduced by anyone outside the editor.
END OF FILE: CHANGES_2026-11-10b.txt

FILE: README.txt (SHA-256 3c2d6f20526bee3048c236f7ab545353a7ca94c35ee2713e5737a54b85cd2901)
O4M RECORD TESTS 2026 v1.0: TEST VECTORS FOR T3 AND T14a (DRAFT PACKAGE, 10 Nov 2026, revision c of the package first issued 7 Nov 2026)

What this is
A small package of test vectors for two of the O4M Record Tests: T3 (chain walk per stream, requirement C3) and T14a (capture counters balance, requirement C14). It holds a reference verifier written in Python with the standard library only, a deterministic generator that rebuilds every vector byte for byte, 55 vectors with their expected verdicts and exit codes, a mutation-kill record, a short script that checks the exit-3 paths, an ambiguity log and a list of proposed reason codes.
Specification: O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23857888 (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23857888). Markdown source SHA-256 8758174e927e971391443ce9142f9c78bafad21ad52f63613d2443a422869d3d.

Status, plainly
This is a self-run. The verifier, the vectors and the mutation harness were written and run by one author, and nobody else has reproduced any result. Nothing here has been verified by anyone outside the editor. The specification is in development and is not an implementation-ready test suite.
This package holds a new reference verifier written from the requirement text. It is not RT-1, the editor's demonstration code mentioned in the specification, and RT-1 is not in this package. The cells for RT-1 in Annex B of the specification stay "not yet checked". MUTATION_RECORD.txt fills the matrix for this new verifier only.

Vector profile v0 is a choice, not a standard
The specification leaves the wire format open; one normative wire profile is planned for its next version. Every format decision these vectors depend on (JSON with JCS rules, one-based numbering, the zero genesis hash, the teardown shape, integer bounds, the reason codes) is a "vector profile v0" choice. Each one is listed in AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt with the reading taken and the vectors that would change. The reason codes are proposed, not normative (REASON_CODES_PROPOSED.txt).

Known limits kept as vectors
t3-012: if a record is altered and every record after it is recomputed, the chain walk returns PASS. A chain walk alone cannot detect a full rewrite from the point of tampering. External commitments made before the rewrite (checkpoints with independent time-stamps and witnesses, C6 and T6a) are needed.
t14a-011: if the operator consistently under-reports what it received, the counters still balance and T14a returns PASS. Only reconciliation against a count the operator does not control (T14c) can show this.
t3-006 (an open stream's tail cannot be checked by T3 alone) is a third limit of the same kind.

How to run
Python 3, no packages to install. On a Mac type python3 instead of python; on Windows, python or py. On 10 Nov 2026 every vector, the exit-code check and two hostile nesting files were run with Python 3.11.18, 3.12.3 and 3.13.18 on Linux, with identical results (the result lines are in MANIFEST.json). The code is written for Python 3.8 or later, but 3.8 to 3.10 have not been run, and Windows and macOS have not been run. From this folder:
  python verify.py T3 vectors/T3/t3-001/input.json
  python verify.py T14a vectors/T14a/t14a-003/input.json
  python verify.py T14a vectors/alt-readings/alt-b-002/input.json --reading B
Exit code 0 is PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 HOLD, 3 a usage error, a file that cannot be read, or an internal error (no verdict). A crash never exits with 1. Input nested deeper than 16 levels is HOLD (INPUT-MALFORMED), decided by a byte scan before parsing, so the verdict does not depend on the Python version. Each vector folder holds input.json, expected.txt (the exact verdict text), expected_exit.txt, about.txt (scenario, attack modelled, expected result and why) and intent.txt (the command and the result the author intended before running the verifier).
To check the package as shipped, first:
  sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS                 (on a Mac: shasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS; every line should say OK)
To rebuild and re-test everything:
  python gen_vectors.py . --expected      (rebuilds inputs and expected outputs; stops on any mismatch with intent)
  python mutation/mutate.py               (runs every mutant against every vector, rewrites MUTATION_RECORD.txt)
  python check_exit_codes.py              (checks the exit-3 paths, which give no verdict and so cannot be vectors)
mutate.py writes the date, time and Python version of your run at the top of MUTATION_RECORD.txt, so after you run it that one file no longer matches SHA256SUMS. Every other file should still match. Compare the mutant lines, not the header.
Extract the zip rather than copying vectors by hand. The evidence hash in every verdict is the SHA-256 of the input.json bytes, so a changed line ending changes every expected verdict. The file .gitattributes stops Git from rewriting line endings. The zip file's own hash is not reproducible (zip stores timestamps); SHA256SUMS is the reference.

Alternative readings
vectors/alt-readings holds vectors for competing readings of "counters balance" and of declared gaps (Readings B, C and D). They are not the primary reading. AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt entries 18 and 20 give the author's recommendation on each.

For regulators, supervisors and anyone handed AI records
REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt indexes the vectors by three questions: is the record complete for the declared scope, who else holds a copy or a count, and how would a missing or changed entry be noticed. It also lists what these vectors cannot show. It is a reading aid, not guidance and not legal advice, and it changes no vector.

Second reader
SECOND_READER_PACKET holds what an independent second implementer should get: instructions, the published specification, and the vector inputs to hand over only after they have written their own verifier. It contains no reference code and no expected outputs. A second reader from the same AI vendor as the author is weak evidence; a different vendor or a human is better. Agreement counts show agreement only.

FILE: REASON_CODES_PROPOSED.txt (SHA-256 5c747ccd04df823390bd4b6dd9d5ce98ac2dccfe86ca4c488b2a9a26facb54fe)
REASON CODES USED BY THE T3 AND T14a VECTORS (vector profile v0, 7 Nov 2026)
Every code below is proposed, not normative. O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0 defines no codes for T3 or T14a; it plans a registry of reason codes in the next version. Result is what the code contributes: an overall result is FAIL if any FAIL code fires, otherwise HOLD if any HOLD code fires, otherwise PASS.

Input level
INPUT-UNPARSEABLE | HOLD | proposed, not normative | the file is not valid UTF-8 JSON
INPUT-DUPKEY | FAIL | proposed, not normative | a key is repeated in an object
INPUT-MALFORMED | HOLD | proposed, not normative | top-level shape wrong, or a string holds a lone surrogate
T2-NFC | FAIL | proposed, not normative | a string is not in Unicode NFC (C2: rejected, never normalised)
DECL-MALFORMED | HOLD | proposed, not normative | the declaration is missing or not in the profile v0 shape
DECLARED-GAP | HOLD | proposed, not normative | the declaration names a gap; that scope is reported as DECLARED GAP, never PASS (V2.3). Under Reading D also raised for counted drops under a declared fail-open policy

T3 (C3, chain walk)
T3-HASH | FAIL | proposed, not normative | stored hash does not match recomputation
T3-START | FAIL | proposed, not normative | lowest seq is not 1, or seq 1 does not carry the zero prev_hash
T3-GAP | FAIL | proposed, not normative | a seq between 1 and the highest seq is missing and not declared
T3-FORK | FAIL | proposed, not normative | two or more records share a stream and seq
T3-LINK | FAIL | proposed, not normative | prev_hash does not match the record at seq minus one (or the nearest lower record present)
T3-TAIL | FAIL | proposed, not normative | teardown missing its correct last_seq, more than one teardown, or records after the teardown
T3-OPEN | HOLD | proposed, not normative | stream has no teardown; tail completeness not established
T3-EMPTY | HOLD | proposed, not normative | input holds no streams
T3-MALFORMED | HOLD | proposed, not normative | a record has missing, extra or wrongly typed fields or bad hex

T14a (C14, counters balance)
T14A-IMBALANCE | FAIL | proposed, not normative | received is not accepted plus dropped
T14A-POLICY | FAIL | proposed, not normative | checkpoint policy differs from the declared fail mode
T14A-UNVERIFIED | HOLD | proposed, not normative | counters labelled UNVERIFIED; can FAIL, never PASS
T14A-NONE | HOLD | proposed, not normative | input holds no checkpoints
T14A-MALFORMED | HOLD | proposed, not normative | missing, non-integer, negative or out-of-range counter, unknown field or repeated checkpoint_id

Alternative readings only
T14A-REGRESS | FAIL | proposed, not normative | Reading B: a counter falls from one checkpoint to the next
T14A-CHAIN | FAIL | proposed, not normative | Reading C: accepted does not equal the records in the chain up to the checkpoint
END OF FILE: REASON_CODES_PROPOSED.txt

FILE: REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt (SHA-256 0c1db860a57b68e2c0aced35e8181adfff6d75f2851cfda799a7800adf957828)
READING AID FOR SUPERVISORS: THREE QUESTIONS ABOUT AI RECORDS, AND WHAT THESE VECTORS SHOW
O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0, tests T3 and T14a, vector profile v0. Added 7 Nov 2026; revised 10 Nov 2026.
For regulators, supervisors, courts, auditors, insurers and anyone handed an operator's AI records.

What this file is
A plain-language index of the vectors in this package, sorted by the question a supervisor would ask. It changes no vector, no expected verdict and no code. It is a reading aid, not a requirement, not guidance from any regulator and not legal advice. The specification (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23857888) is the authority; where this file and the specification differ, the specification wins.

The three questions
1. Is the record complete for the scope the operator declared?
2. Who else holds a copy, or a count, that the operator does not control?
3. If an entry were missing or changed, how would anyone notice?

How to read a result
PASS means the test found nothing wrong in what it was given. It does not mean the record is true, complete beyond its declared scope, or approved. Every verdict in this package ends with "Scope 5: integrity is not truth; an intact record of false content is still false content."
FAIL means the test found a specific problem and names it with a reason code (proposed, not normative).
HOLD means the test could not reach PASS or FAIL: something is missing, malformed or declared as a gap. A HOLD is never a PASS.
Run any vector yourself with Python 3 and no packages: python verify.py T3 vectors/T3/t3-003/input.json

QUESTION 1. IS THE RECORD COMPLETE FOR THE DECLARED SCOPE?
A record can only be complete against something written down in advance. Each verdict prints the declaration first (action types, tolerances, fail mode, declared gaps).
t3-001 | PASS | an untampered stream: what "complete for the declared scope" looks like when the test can show it
t3-019 | HOLD | the operator declared a gap in advance and the record skips exactly that entry: reported as a DECLARED GAP, never PASS
t3-020 | FAIL | a gap was declared, but a different entry is missing: a declaration does not excuse what it does not name
t3-006 | HOLD | the stream was never closed, so nobody can tell whether entries after the last one existed
t3-013 | HOLD | no records at all: the test refuses to call nothing a PASS
t14a-008 | HOLD | no counters at all: same rule for the counting test
t14a-016 | FAIL | the declaration says recording stops on failure (fail-closed), but the counters say it carried on and dropped entries (fail-open)
t14a-002 | PASS | entries were dropped and counted under a declared fail-open policy; alt-d-001 (HOLD) shows the stricter reading the author recommends

QUESTION 2. WHO ELSE HOLDS A COPY, OR A COUNT?
T3 and T14a work on what the operator hands over. Three vectors are kept on purpose to show what they cannot see without something held by someone else.
t3-012 | PASS | KNOWN LIMIT: an entry was changed and everything after it was recomputed. The chain still checks. Only a copy committed earlier by someone else (checkpoints with independent time-stamps and witnesses, requirement C6, test T6a) can show this.
t14a-011 | PASS | KNOWN LIMIT: the operator under-reports what it received, consistently. The counters still balance. Only a count the operator does not control (test T14c) can show this.
t3-006 | HOLD | KNOWN LIMIT: an open stream's tail. Agent start and stop registration (requirement C13) is needed.
t14a-017 | HOLD | counters labelled UNVERIFIED: a check resting on an unverified copy can FAIL, never PASS
t14a-018 | PASS | the operator's own file says its counters were "independently reconciled". The verdict prints this as "claimed in input, not checked": an operator's statement about independence is not independence.

QUESTION 3. IF AN ENTRY WERE MISSING OR CHANGED, HOW WOULD ANYONE NOTICE?
Deletions
t3-003 | FAIL | one entry deleted from the middle: the numbering gap and the broken link both show
t3-004 | FAIL | the first entry deleted
t3-005 | FAIL | the last entry deleted: the closing record still points at it
t14a-003 | FAIL | five entries vanished without being counted
t14a-006 | FAIL | one checkpoint out of five is short: the verdict names that checkpoint only
t14a-019 | FAIL | exactly one entry lost: the smallest possible gap still fails
alt-c-002 | FAIL | (alternative reading C) counters balance on their own, but do not match the entries actually in the chain
Changes, insertions and re-ordering
t3-010 | FAIL | the content of one entry edited
t3-011 | FAIL | one entry edited and its own fingerprint recomputed, but not the ones after it
t3-007 | FAIL | two entries swapped
t3-008 | FAIL | an extra entry inserted with a valid fingerprint
t3-009 | FAIL | the record forks into two versions
t3-026 | FAIL | an entry copied from one stream into another
t3-017, t3-024, t3-025, t3-027 | FAIL | the closing record misstates the end, or entries appear after it
alt-b-002 | FAIL | (alternative reading B) a counter goes backwards between checkpoints, a cheap way to hide a loss

WHEN TWO TOOLS COULD READ THE SAME FILE DIFFERENTLY
These matter to anyone comparing results from different tools. A lax reader could reach a different verdict on the same bytes.
t3-021 | FAIL | the same key appears twice in one entry, so two readers could see two different records
t3-018 | FAIL | text stored in a non-standard Unicode form that looks identical on screen
t3-022, t3-023 | HOLD | a fingerprint in upper case; a number written as text
t14a-012, t14a-013, t14a-014 | HOLD | a counter written as true, as 100.0, or one above the largest whole number many readers hold exactly
t3-014 | FAIL | numbering that starts at 0 instead of 1: a profile choice, logged in AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt entry 7
t3-029, t3-031 | HOLD | a file nested deeper than 16 levels, or exactly 16: the limit is fixed so that no tool's verdict depends on its programming language (entry 36)
t3-030 | HOLD | a file saved with a byte order mark at the start (entry 35)

What none of these vectors can tell you
Whether the operator recorded everything it did (C13, C14 with T14c, C22). Whether a record was made at the time it claims (C6, C15). Who signed it and whether the key was safe (C5, C10, C23). Whether what the record says is true (C17, and evidence from others).
Status: self-run by one author, generated with AI assistance, not reproduced by anyone else. See README.txt.
END OF FILE: REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt

FILE: SECOND_READER_PACKET/INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_SECOND_READER_2026-11-10.txt (SHA-256 f31c407edae930ab9c8f9382ec7c2d0b0d47d20ce2af3ea0d2ff77aa72437c30)
INSTRUCTIONS FOR THE SECOND READER: T3 and T14a, O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0
Version 2, 10 Nov 2026 (replaces the 7 Nov 2026 version). For a fresh chat on a different AI vendor from the author, or for a human.

For the person handing this over (Gareth): hand it over in three stages, using the three zip files.
Stage 1: this file and the specification file only.
Stage 2: the folder pass2_inputs, only after the reader says Pass 1 is finished and has written SECOND_READER_LOG.txt.
Stage 3: the author's expected files, only after the reader has saved all Pass 2 verdicts.
Never give the reader verify.py, gen_vectors.py, mutation/, check_exit_codes.py, AMBIGUITY_LOG.txt, REASON_CODES_PROPOSED.txt, REGULATOR_QUESTIONS.txt, the about.txt or intent.txt files, the repository link, or any Cowork brief.

What you have
The specification, O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0, in the file "O4M Record Tests 2026 v1_0 2026-11-01.md". Its SHA-256 must be 8758174e927e971391443ce9142f9c78bafad21ad52f63613d2443a422869d3d (published at DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23857888). Check it before you start. The parts that matter most are: Conventions, Three profiles, the C2, C3, C13 and C14 rows, Scope, V2.1 (the common output rule and A1 T1b-VACUITY), V2.3 and V2.4.

Your task
Pass 1. Write your own verifier for T3 and T14a from the specification alone, in Python with the standard library only. It takes a test name and a file path and prints a verdict; choose exit codes and say what they are. The specification does not fix an input format, so you will have to decide one; when you receive the inputs in Pass 2 you may adapt your reader to their format, and log that you did. Before running anything, write SECOND_READER_LOG.txt listing every place the text left you a choice: the section, the question, and the choice you made. The verdict layout is part of what you must work out from the common output rule.
Pass 2. You now receive pass2_inputs (50 files). Each file name ends with the test it belongs to (__T3 or __T14a). Run your verifier on each and save each verdict and exit code in a folder. Some inputs are broken or hostile on purpose (for example very deeply nested, or saved with an odd first character). If your verifier crashes or does something you did not plan on one of them, record what happened and the exit code. Do not fix it quietly: log it as a choice the text left open or as your own bug.
Pass 3. You now receive the author's expected files, named <vector id>__expected.txt and <vector id>__expected_exit.txt. For each vector where you disagree, write one line: vector id, which side differs, and why (text unclear, a vector or profile choice not stated in the text, your bug, or the author's bug). Then write a short summary: how many vectors agree on result (PASS, FAIL or HOLD), how many agree on reason codes (expect few, since the specification names no codes for these tests), and the five most important ambiguities.

Rules
Never ask for the reference code. Never edit your verifier to match the expected output without logging the change and the reason. Do not search the web for these tests, their repository or other people's versions of them: the point is what the specification alone lets you do. You publish nothing and contact nobody. Your log is the product; a count of agreements shows agreement only.
When you finish, send the log and the Pass 3 summary back as plain text.
END OF FILE: SECOND_READER_PACKET/INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_SECOND_READER_2026-11-10.txt

FILE: SECOND_READER_PACKET/O4M Record Tests 2026 v1_0 2026-11-01.md (SHA-256 8758174e927e971391443ce9142f9c78bafad21ad52f63613d2443a422869d3d)
# O4M Record Tests 2026 (v1.0)
## Open tests for checking AI activity records from outside: requirements C1 to C23

Public comment draft, published Sun 1 Nov 2026. Comments close Tue 1 Dec 2026.
Status: a specification in development, with demonstration code that has not yet been reproduced by anyone outside the editor. It is not an implementation-ready test suite.
Drafting history: circulated in draft as v1.1 (3 Oct 2026), v1.1.1 and v1.1.2 (31 Oct to 1 Nov 2026). Requirements C1 to C19 first appeared in DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23546436 (22 Oct 2026). The drafts were reviewed by the editor, by two AI models from other vendors (OpenAI ChatGPT and Google Gemini) and against a self-reported demonstrator. The change lists below are kept so reviewers can see what moved.
Copyright 2026 Gareth Wong. Licensed under CC BY 4.0. Intended for assignment to the O4M association. Editor: Gareth Wong.
DOI of this version: 10.5281/zenodo.23857888 (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23857888).
Prior public record: DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23546436 (22 Oct 2026, gap brief v1.7 and evidence table v3.3).
Comment window: 1 Nov to 1 Dec 2026. Corrections are published as numbered new versions; earlier versions stay online.
Comments until 1 Dec 2026 to comments@sts-forensics.com (subject 'O4M Record Tests'). Comments may be published with the commenter's name unless they ask otherwise. Comments and the editor's replies are kept in a public comment log.

## What this is
A set of 23 written requirements (C1 to C23) and tests that let someone outside an AI system check two things about its activity records: that they are complete for the scope the operator declared, and that they have not been altered since they were written. It is a verifier test suite that sits beside existing receipt and logging formats rather than replacing them. It checks records. It does not check whether the AI system was right, safe, lawful or well governed.

At this release no system can obtain PASS on every in-scope requirement. Expect HOLD, with reason codes, on C22 item 4, C23 item 3 and T14e until the named dependencies exist (independent time-stamping in two jurisdictions, three independent verifiers, an independent billing or egress source). A HOLD with reason codes is the informative output.

These tests are intended to run as a records-integrity profile alongside existing schemes (for example AIUC-1 logging controls, ISO/IEC 24970, OVERT receipts, SCITT, TRACE). Mappings are published as they are completed. No clause-level claim about any of those documents is made in this release.

## How this relates to other work
Other open suites publish receipt or evidence test vectors, for example draft-gruszka-signed-evaluation-receipts-04, draft-watts-eqr-conformance-03, draft-gravit-gevp-11 and draft-msebenzi-evidence-action-00; SCITT (RFC 9943, RFC 9942) covers receipts with inclusion proofs. Format checks such as canonical encoding, signatures, hash chains and reproduction are common to these drafts, and no novelty is claimed for them here. The tests in this suite focus on omission: counters, reconciliation against counts the operator does not control, coverage and liveness, clock independence, public-randomness sampling and model-identity grading. Where a test has prior art, it is cited (T1b-VACUITY builds on the non-empty rule in draft-watts-eqr-conformance-03 section 4). ISO/IEC 24970 (AI system logging) is at final-draft stage; a mapping will follow once its text is available.

## Who it is for
People who must rely on AI activity records they did not produce: auditors and assessors, claims and disputes teams, regulators' technical staff, researchers, and builders of receipt or logging formats who want an outside test of their own claims.

## What is in this release
1. Requirement text for C1 to C23 (draft v1.1 text, with the changes listed below).
2. Test descriptions for every requirement, each with a status: Demo, Spec or Blocked.
3. New since draft v1.1 (section V2): the common output rule; T1b-VACUITY; clock grades; sampling power; T21c-GRIND; T14e-SHADOW; T4b-SPOOF; the C7 model-identity ladder with C7-D; the declared-gap reporting rule; unsigned agreement in place of signed verdicts (C23 item 3, T-AGREE3).
4. Annex A: legal-effect crosswalk (grade A sources only).
5. Annex B: mutation-kill matrix (honest state: not yet checked).
Further candidate requirements exist in internal draft. They are not part of this release and are not commitments.
No test vectors are published in this release. Vectors for the Demo rows follow, each with its mutation-kill record. Test vectors for T3 and T14a are planned before Sun 15 Nov 2026.

## Changes in draft v1.1.2 (from draft v1.1.1; no requirement added or removed)
- Name use: the example statement now includes the T1b strength line, which the same paragraph requires.
- V2.4: the banned word "seal" now applies to describing the result itself. C18 write-ahead seals are reported under their test name.
- A2 clock grade: grade C now covers every claim whose seal time comes from the operator's clock, whatever the effect source. v1.1.1 left that case ungraded.
- A6 T4b-SPOOF: where the operator runs both the model transcript and the tool log, an unpaired record is still FAIL. Only a PASS is reduced to HOLD, because a mismatch is evidence whoever holds the log.
- T-AGREE3: fewer than two matching results is now HOLD (VERIFIER-DISAGREE), not FAIL. Disagreement between verifiers says nothing about the operator's records. All results are listed.
- T-SAMP1: clarified as a test of the verifier's sampling method over repeated trials, not a per-export test.
- Status corrected: Demo is now 8 (two partial). Every Demo row runs in the RT-1 demonstrator (1 Nov 2026). C13 and C19 move to Spec because their earlier demonstration was not re-run.
- C12 corrected. v1.1.1 presented the PLD expiry periods (Art. 17: 10 years, 25 for latent personal injury) as retention periods. The PLD imposes no retention duty. Retention is now operator-declared with a stated basis.
- C2: strings MUST be NFC before encoding; a verifier rejects non-NFC input rather than normalising it. RFC 8785 (JCS) does not normalise Unicode itself.
- C14: every counter in a verdict carries a provenance label (SOURCE-LOCAL, INDEPENDENTLY-RECONCILED or UNVERIFIED). Counters produced by the capture point are SOURCE-LOCAL, however well they balance.
- C19: re-time-stamping after an outage never restores original-time assurance.
- Profile W: every PASS under Profile W prints "integrity of received records; source completeness not established".
- Independent sources: a source counts as independent only if the verifier obtains it from its producer or it carries the producer's signature. A copy delivered by the operator is labelled UNVERIFIED and cannot turn a HOLD into a PASS.

## Changes in draft v1.1.1 (from draft v1.1 of 3 Oct 2026)
- C7 replaced by the graded model-identity ladder (C7-0 to C7-5), identifier precision and C7-D change visibility.
- C23 item 3 and T-KEY3 replaced by unsigned independent agreement (T-AGREE3). No verdict is signed.
- C19 read as a time-source quorum; every ordering claim carries a clock grade; T6c (independent clock) is OPEN.
- C20 wording: "before any public claim that two implementations agree".
- New tests: T1b-VACUITY (C1), T14e-SHADOW (C14), T21c-GRIND (C21), T4b-SPOOF (C4); sampling-power printing for every sampled check.
- Text refers to "the verifier" throughout; any party may act as verifier.
