## Status of the 23 requirements at release
Demo = runs for the editor against synthetic records in the RT-1 demonstrator (1 Nov 2026). The demonstrator is not published in this release (code and frozen vectors are planned before Sun 15 Nov 2026), and no Demo result has been reproduced by anyone else. Spec = written, no runnable test yet. Blocked = needs something from outside.
Demo (8, two partial): C2, C3, C5, C6 (partial; T6c open), C11, C14 (T14a only), C16 (split view only), C18.
Also run in RT-1 on synthetic records, but still Spec until vectors are published: T1b fixtures, T4, T4b, T17 against a simulated effect log, T21c, T22a, a nonce liveness check (planned for C22 in v1.2) and T-AGREE3.
Spec: C1, C4, C7, C8, C9, C10, C12, C13, C15, C17 (simulated effect log only), C19, C20, C21, C22, C23.
Blocked items: Profile P (provider-signed receipts); C8 and C17 on vendor-hosted AI; C17 real test needs an effect-source partner.

## Conventions

- **MUST / SHOULD** follow RFC 2119.
- **"Verifier"** means a party holding only the export bundle (C11) plus public material (the anchor and witness keys, and the published checkpoints), plus any independent sources named in the verdict.
- **"Independent source"** (an effect log, tool-side log, nonce monitor, billing or egress record) counts as independent only if the verifier obtains it from its producer, or it carries the producer's signature under a key the operator does not hold. A copy delivered inside the operator's export is labelled UNVERIFIED, and a check that rests on it can return FAIL but never PASS.
- **"Consequential action"** means an action with an external effect that the operator has declared in C1 as consequential (for example a payment, a message sent outside the organisation, a change to a production system).
- **Status** means how the test stands at this release:
  - **Demo** = a runnable test exists in the editor's demonstration code against synthetic records. Demonstration code and vectors are not published in this release.
  - **Spec** = written here, not yet coded.
  - **Blocked** = cannot be tested until someone outside the verifier provides something.

## Three profiles

C1 to C23 cannot be met in full by most organisations, because most do not control the runtime their AI runs in. Results are therefore reported against a **profile**, and the profile is printed on every result.

| Profile | Who can meet it | What it proves | Requirements in scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W: witness of vendor records** | Anyone using AI through a vendor that offers an admin export, for example (where offered): Microsoft 365 Copilot (Graph interaction export), ChatGPT Enterprise and Claude Enterprise (Compliance APIs), the Gemini app (Vault), Copilot Studio, Agentforce, Dialogflow CX, Lex. Small firms included | "These records have not changed since the verifier received them, none received was dropped, and here is exactly when the verifier was and was not receiving." It **cannot** prove they were complete at source | C2, C4 (payloads hashed at ingestion), C6, C10, C11, C12, C14 (ingestion counters, and provider reconciliation where offered), C15 (ingestion time), C16, C19, C20, C21, **C22 items 4, 5 and 6**, **C23 items 1, 3 and 4** (the verifier's own keys and infrastructure; item 2 SHOULD where the customer agrees to canaries) |
| **F: full capture** | Organisations that run their own agents or bots on infrastructure they control: API-built apps, self-hosted models, agent frameworks | Everything W proves, plus capture at the source, execution witnessing, write-ahead sealing, liveness at the capture point and key custody outside the operator's credential domain. Completeness is bounded by independent counts where they exist | C1 to C23 |
| **P: provider-assisted** | Only possible if model providers and platforms add signed per-request usage receipts and signed export feeds. **Nobody offers this today** | Completeness against the provider's own signed count, closing the targeted-omission gap T-AAL1 (a reconciliation test showing that bulk omission is detectable and targeted omission is not) found | C14 at request level, C17 at the provider boundary, on top of F |

Every PASS under Profile W prints "integrity of received records; source completeness not established". This line is part of the result, not commentary.

Profile W is the product for small and mid-sized firms. F is for design partners. P is a standards and regulatory ask, not a product.

## The requirements and their tests

**Data availability key:**
- **S** = small firm on SaaS chat
- **M** = mid firm with its own API apps
- **L** = large enterprise or platform
- **Y** = obtainable today
- **Partly** = only with engineering effort or on some stacks
- **N** = not obtainable

| # | Requirement (current text, short form) | Level | Test ID and method (verifier side) | Pass criterion | Attack cases | Status | S / M / L |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **(amended 3 Oct)** Pre-register policy, software digest, fail mode, checkpoint interval, clock tolerance and witness set, sealed before the first event. **Also declare: heartbeat interval H and missed-heartbeat limit k (C22); seal latency budget; capture-daemon CPU, memory and disk reservation; the fallback rule when a budget is exceeded; the list of consequential action types; and, under Profile W, the ingestion recovery window.** Test runs also pre-register the expected event count | MUST | T1: the pre-registration record is the first leaf, its seal time precedes every event, and the declared parameters match the observed ones | All declared parameters present; none changed without a signed amendment event | Retroactive policy change; missing pre-registration; budgets declared after an incident | Spec | N / Y / Y |
| C2 | Deterministic canonical encoding (deterministic CBOR or JCS); strings MUST be NFC before encoding, and a verifier rejects non-NFC input rather than normalising it (JCS does not normalise Unicode) | MUST | T2: re-encode each record and compare the bytes and hash | 100% byte-identical | Non-canonical re-serialisation to alter hashes | Demo (RT-1: T2) | Y (the verifier encodes on ingestion) / Y / Y |
| C3 | Sequence number and previous-record hash per stream, assigned at the source | MUST | T3: chain walk per stream; no gaps, no forks; teardown carries last_seq | Contiguous sequence, valid links | Deletion, reorder, insertion | Demo (RT-1: T3) | **N** at the source (vendor-assigned; the verifier assigns on ingestion under W) / Y / Y |
| C4 | Salted commitments to inputs, outputs, tool calls and results; payloads held apart | MUST | T4: for sampled records, the opened payload plus salt reproduces the commitment | All sampled openings match | Payload swap after commit | Spec | Partly (hash on ingestion) / Y / Y |
| C5 | Signature over every record, key held apart from the application; at scale the capture point signs | MUST | T5: verify every signature against a key registered under C10 | 100% valid | Forged records; key misuse | Demo | N at source (the verifier signs on ingestion under W) / Y / Y |
| C6 | Checkpoints (Merkle root every N records or T seconds, one per interval per shard), time from an outside TSA or QTSP, cosigned by a quorum of stateful witnesses. **The time is the anchor's or witness's own clock reading.** A gap longer than the declared maximum interval is a finding | MUST | T6a: each checkpoint carries a valid RFC 3161 token or QTSP stamp and a quorum of witness cosignatures. T6b: consecutive checkpoint gaps are at most the declared maximum. T6c: the token time is independent of the operator's clock | All three pass | Split view, backdating across a checkpoint, operator clock skew | Demo (RT-1: witnessed checkpoints; no time-stamping service used). **T6c open**: the demo time sources are the operator's clock | Y / Y / Y |
| C7 | Model identity: graded ladder C7-0 to C7-5 with identifier precision and change visibility (C7-D). Full text in section V2 (replaces the v1.1 text) | MUST for black-box tests | T7: report the highest C7 state supported, the identifier precision, C7-D, and what the next state needs. T7-PREC: a verifier output missing precision or C7-D fails. | state reported with all qualifiers; never FAIL for a low state. | silent model swap (visible only at C7-4 or C7-5, or at pinned-version precision). | Spec | Partly (vendor exports name the model) / Y / Y |
| C8 | Runtime attestation (TPM quote, SEV-SNP/TDX report, DICE chain), bound per checkpoint interval | MUST where the workload can run code near its own capture path; SHOULD otherwise | T8: an attestation per interval verifies to the hardware root and matches the pre-registered measurement | Every interval attested | Capture-point subversion | Spec | N / Partly / Partly |
| C9 | Authority records, including the delegation chain from human to workload to agent | SHOULD for actions | T9: each consequential action links to an authority record whose chain resolves to a named human or role | Chain complete | Unauthorised action; orphan agent | Spec | Partly (Entra/IdP logs) / Y / Y |
| C10 | Key lifecycle: issued before the first record, revocation, rotation log | MUST | T10: every signing key was issued before first use, never used after revocation, and every rotation is logged | Clean lifecycle | Back-issued keys | Spec | Y (the verifier keys) / Y / Y |
| C11 | Export bundle with manifest and export ID; targeted exports with inclusion and consistency proofs | MUST | T11: manifest hash valid; each included record has an inclusion proof to a cosigned checkpoint; consistency proofs link checkpoints | All proofs verify | Cherry-picked export | Demo | Y / Y / Y |
| C12 | **(corrected 1 Nov)** Retention period and its basis (legal, contractual or evidential) declared by the operator in C1; deletion only as a signed, logged event. Limitation and expiry periods (for example PLD Art. 16, three years from knowledge, and Art. 17, ten years, 25 for latent personal injury) inform the declared period but are not retention duties, and personal data stays subject to data protection law | MUST | T12: the retention policy is pre-registered (C1); deletions appear as signed events; sampled old records are retrievable | No unsigned gaps | Silent deletion | Spec | Y (the verifier custody) / Y / Y |
| C13 | Agent lifecycle under workload identity (SPIFFE; WIMSE is still an IETF draft); spawn and teardown on a never-shed lane; teardown carries last_seq | MUST where agents spawn agents | T13: every agent ID has a spawn record signed under a registered workload identity, and a teardown with last_seq | No unregistered agents | Sybil keys; suppressed teardown | Spec (an earlier demonstration was not re-run on 1 Nov) | N / Partly / Y |
| C14 | Capture completeness: signed received/accepted/dropped counters in every checkpoint; declared fail-open or fail-closed policy; credentials held only at the capture point, egress denied elsewhere; enumerated IPC and egress surfaces. Every model-call record carries the provider request ID and the provider-reported usage copied from the response. Reconcile per usage bucket against the provider's usage source, with a declared tolerance | MUST (counters, policy, request ID, reconciliation where a provider usage source exists); SHOULD (custody, surface enumeration) | T14a: counters balance. T14b: drops fail under fail-closed. T14c (T-AAL1): logged usage against provider buckets, within tolerance. T14d: no two records share a request ID; request IDs are well-formed. Every counter in the verdict carries a provenance label: SOURCE-LOCAL (produced by the capture point), INDEPENDENTLY-RECONCILED, or UNVERIFIED | Counters balance; reconciliation within tolerance on every bucket | Flood, hidden drops, bypass, operator omission, padding | Demo (RT-1: T14a only; T14c not re-run). **T14c detects volume omission only; targeted omission needs Profile P** | Partly (provider usage APIs need admin keys) / Y / Y |
| C15 | Time: declared tolerance and reference (NTS, PTP), periodic signed time samples (such as Roughtime), causal reference to the triggering record | MUST (tolerance, reference); SHOULD (causal link) | T15: signed time samples bracket each interval; record times fall within tolerance; causal references point backwards | Within tolerance | Backdating within an interval (known residual) | Spec | Y (ingestion time) / Y / Y |
| C16 | Split-view resistance: verifiers compare checkpoints (gossip); checkpoints published; a monitor consumes the stream and trips within a declared time | MUST | T16a: gossip comparison finds no fork. T16b: an injected anomaly trips the monitor within the declared time | No fork; trip within time | Split view; slow detection | Demo (RT-1: comparison with published checkpoints finds split views). **T16b spec** | Y / Y / Y |
| C17 | Execution witness: the executing component signs actual input and output with a key outside the workload's namespace; its measurement is bound into the checkpoint. **(clarified 3 Oct) A conforming test compares against an independent effect source (for example a payment processor's or bank's record), never only a lab oracle. Out of scope under Profile W** | MUST where actions are consequential | T17: each consequential action has an executor signature and the executor measurement matches C1; each is matched to an independent effect source record | All actions witnessed; no effect without a record | Subverted executor; spoofed transcript | Spec (RT-1 checks against a simulated effect log only). **A real test needs an effect source** | N / Partly / Partly |
| C18 | Write-ahead sealing in a separate trust domain: sealed before the external effect, never waiting on the network; self-termination is a sealed event; the seal chain head is committed in every checkpoint; the seal key is outside the operator's and workload's reach | MUST where actions are consequential | T18: every effected action has a seal earlier than its effect; the seal chain is contiguous; the head matches the checkpoint; the record body equals the sealed body | All pass | Death before record; edit before re-time-stamping | Demo (RT-1: T18, seal before effect) | N / Partly / Y |
| C19 | Anchor availability (time-source quorum; ordering is clock grade C until T6c closes): declared quorum of independent anchors (**see C22 item 4 for the minimum diversity**); an outage is a signed event with a declared maximum; records from a window without an independent time-stamp are re-time-stamped and flagged as lower assurance, never dropped; re-time-stamping never restores original-time assurance. Fail-closed withholds consequential effects only once the declared maximum outage is exceeded | MUST | T19: outage events present; re-time-stamping complete; the flag carries through to the verdict; no outage exceeds the maximum without withholding | All pass | Anchor DDoS | Spec (an earlier demonstration was not re-run on 1 Nov) | Y / Y / Y |
| C20 | Open, reproducible verifier: open-source reference verifier, reproducible build, published digest; a second spec-only implementation agrees on the published vectors before any public claim that two implementations agree; formal verification of the core checks as a roadmap item | MUST (open source, reproducible); SHOULD (second implementation, formal verification) | T20a: two builds give an identical digest. T20b: the second implementation matches every vector verdict | Identical digests and verdicts | Verifier bug or backdoor | Spec | Y (a duty on the verifier, not the customer) |
| C21 | Verifier independence: disclosure of financial relationships with the operator, format vendors and relying insurers; samples drawn with public randomness fixed after the export is sealed (for example a drand round); the operator cannot choose a verifier after seeing a result | MUST | T21a: the sample is reproducible from the beacon round and export ID. T21b: a disclosure statement is present in every verdict. **T-SAMP1** (see below) | Reproducible sample; disclosure present; T-SAMP1 passes | Cherry-picked sampling; auditor shopping; sample prediction | Spec | Y (duty on the verifier) |
| **C22** | **(new 3 Oct) Capture liveness and availability.** (1) **Heartbeat:** the capture point emits a signed heartbeat every H seconds (declared in C1); missing heartbeats beyond k × H are a finding in their own right. (2) **Dead man's switch:** if the heartbeat stops, or seal latency exceeds the declared budget, consequential actions are withheld until capture is restored; non-consequential actions may continue only under a signed fail-open notice. (3) **Resource reservation:** the capture and seal daemon runs with reserved CPU, memory and disk (cgroups or equivalent) that the workload cannot consume, declared in C1 with the latency budget. (4) **Anchor diversity:** at least two timestamp authorities in different jurisdictions (one SHOULD be an EU qualified trust service provider), and checkpoints published to at least two independent transparency logs. (5) **Ingestion continuity (Profile W):** the verifier checks vendor export identifiers and timestamps for gaps, and declares a recovery window shorter than the vendor's retention. (6) **Coverage statement:** every verdict prints the windows when capture was live, degraded and absent; a verdict never silently spans a gap | MUST where actions are consequential; SHOULD otherwise. Under Profile W, items 4, 5 and 6 are MUST | T-DOS1 to T-DOS6 (see below); plus T22a: every verdict carries a coverage statement whose windows reconcile with the heartbeat and checkpoint record | All T-DOS tests pass; coverage statement present and consistent | Starve, kill or flood the capture daemon; DDoS a TSA or log; block the verifier ingestion; bury consequential actions in junk | Spec | Partly (items 4 to 6 only) / Y / Y |
| **C23** | **(new 3 Oct) Key and verifier custody isolation.** (1) No signing, witness or anchor key sits in any credential store, secret manager or account that the monitored workload, or its operator's automation, can read. Acceptable custody: a separate legal entity, a QTSP, or hardware with attested policy outside the operator's cloud account. (2) **Canary credentials** are planted in the operator's secret stores; any use is a sealed, alarming event. (3) **Independent agreement:** at least two of three independent verifiers run the same suite version on the same evidence and publish their results unsigned, each with the suite version, the verifier name and version, and the SHA-256 of the evidence bundle. Signing of verdicts is deferred and is not part of this release. (4) Key compromise has a pre-registered response: revoke, restart from the last independently cosigned checkpoint, and mark everything after it as lower assurance | MUST where agents can act on infrastructure; items 1, 3 and 4 MUST for the verifier under every profile; item 2 SHOULD under Profile W | T-KEY1, T-KEY2, T-AGREE3 (see below); plus T23a: the pre-registered compromise response exists and names the last-good checkpoint rule | All T-KEY tests pass; response pre-registered | Agent reads operator secrets and signs records; canary use unnoticed; one verifier compromised | Spec | Y for the verifier's own keys / Partly (canaries need operator agreement) / Y |

## Tests added in draft v1.1

All are Spec status. T-DOS1 to T-DOS6, T-KEY1 and T-KEY2 are fault tests run by the operator; their results are self-reported unless an independent party witnesses the run, and the verdict says which.

**C22: T-DOS1 to T-DOS6**

| Test | Scenario | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|
| T-DOS1 | Workload exhausts CPU and memory on the host | Capture daemon keeps sealing within its budget (reservation works), or consequential actions are withheld |
| T-DOS2 | Capture daemon is killed | Heartbeat gap detected within k × H; consequential actions withheld; the kill is a sealed event or a detected gap |
| T-DOS3 | One of two TSAs is down for 2 hours | Checkpoints continue on the other; outage signed; no window without an independent time-stamp |
| T-DOS4 | Both TSAs are down beyond the maximum outage | Fail-closed for consequential actions; records re-time-stamped on recovery and flagged lower assurance |
| T-DOS5 | the verifier ingestion blocked for 48 hours (Profile W) | Recovered from the vendor export with no ID gap; the coverage statement shows the delay; where vendor retention is 30 days, a gap longer than 30 days is a permanent, printed loss |
| T-DOS6 | A flood of 100x junk records alongside 10 consequential actions | All 10 sealed on the priority lane within budget; junk counted under C14 |

**C23: T-KEY1, T-KEY2, T-AGREE3**

| Test | Scenario | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|
| T-KEY1 | An agent obtains every secret in the operator's account | It cannot produce a record, checkpoint or verdict that verifies |
| T-KEY2 | A canary credential is used | Alarm and sealed event within the declared time |
| T-AGREE3 (replaces T-KEY3) | Three published unsigned results exist for the same evidence bundle hash and suite version | PASS if at least two match on result and reason codes; HOLD (VERIFIER-DISAGREE) if fewer than two match, listing all three; HOLD if fewer than three results are available. No signatures are checked |

**C21: T-SAMP1**

| Test | Scenario | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|
| T-SAMP1 | An attacker knows the full method but not the drand round, and tampers only with records it predicts will not be sampled | Tampered records are sampled at the rate expected for a random sample (statistical test at a declared significance level); no advantage from knowing the method. This tests the verifier's sampling method over repeated trials on synthetic exports; it is not run on each export |

## Scope (printed on every verdict)

1. **What this covers.** C1 to C23 covers records of AI system operation at inference and action time. It does not cover training provenance, compute accounting for training, model internals, or behavioural tests for deception or sandbagging.
2. **Policy adequacy is out of scope.** A verdict says what happened and whether the records of it are intact and complete within the stated coverage. It does not say whether the operator's policy was wise, lawful or sufficient. That is a human or auditor judgement.
3. **No Assurance Level claim.** No verdict is a claim to any AI Assurance Level (Brundage et al., arXiv:2601.11699) or to any Attestation Assurance Level or conformance level of OVERT (Glacis Technologies, overt.is). Any mapping to those schemes is informative only.
4. **Evidence, never containment.** The tests do not prevent anything. They make suppression, tampering and gaps visible and attributable, after the fact, to a party other than the operator. Some requirements ask the operator to withhold actions when capture fails; the tests only check, after the fact, whether that happened. Containment and security remain the operator's job.
5. **Integrity is not truth.** An intact record of false content is still false content. C17 with an independent effect source is the only check on content against the world.

## What exists and what is missing

| Item | State |
|---|---|
| Runnable tests | 8 of 23 requirements have at least one demo test in RT-1: C2, C3, C5, C6 (partly), C11, C14 (T14a), C16 (split view), C18. C17 has a simulated effect-log check only |
| Written but not coded | C1, C4, C7, C9, C10, C12, C13, C15 (within interval), C16b, C20, C21 (with T-SAMP1), C22 (T-DOS1 to T-DOS6, T22a), C23 (T-KEY1, T-KEY2, T-AGREE3, T23a), T6c |
| Blocked from outside | Profile P (provider-signed per-request receipts); C8 and C17 on vendor-hosted AI (only the vendor can do them); C17 real test needs an effect-source partner |
| Test vectors | None published yet. C20 requires publishing them with the reference verifier |
| Operational cost to a verifier | C23 item 3 needs three independent verifiers. C22 item 4 needs two TSAs in different jurisdictions (one an EU QTSP) and two transparency logs. Neither exists yet |

## Honest ceiling (unchanged, restated)

An operator that controls the capture point can omit records. Only three things narrow this:
- independent counts (C14; T-AAL1 shows the limit);
- hardware binding (C8, C17);
- provider-signed receipts (Profile P).

Under Profile W, the verifier proves custody from the moment it receives the records, prints exactly when it was and was not receiving them (C22 item 6), and says so.


## V2. NEW SINCE DRAFT v1.1

### V2.1 Output, vacuity, clocks, sampling, grinding, shadow channels, spoofed tool results
Common output rule for every verdict (applies to every test)
Every verdict prints, in this order: (1) a declaration-strength line (T1b-VACUITY); (2) the clock grade of each ordering claim (A2); (3) for any sampled check, n, s, the formula and the detection chances (A3); (4) the result PASS, FAIL or HOLD with reason codes, and the verifier's disclosure statement including "fee fixed before the run; not contingent on the result", or "no fee" for volunteer verifiers; (5) the line "Unsigned result. No certificates are issued."; (6) "T6c: OPEN" until T6c is closed.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A1. T1b-VACUITY (under C1)
Scenario: The operator's declaration sets the scope every other test checks against. If the declaration lists no consequential action types, or sets tolerances so loose that nothing could fail, every test passes without testing anything.
Test: Before any other test runs, the verifier reads the declaration and prints its strength on the first line of the verdict: number of consequential action types declared; tolerances (maximum allowed gap, maximum clock skew, maximum unsealed window); fail mode (fail-closed or fail-open); monitor set (which monitors are declared and who runs them); declared gaps: [list], covering [n of m] consequential action types.
Pass criterion: The test never returns PASS on its own. It returns "DECLARATION RECORDED" and lets the suite proceed only if at least one consequential action type is declared, every tolerance is finite and within the bounds in the profile (default bounds for this release: maximum uncounted gap 0, maximum clock skew 5 s, maximum unsealed window 60 s; a profile may tighten them), and a fail mode is stated. Otherwise the whole verdict is HOLD with reason code DECL-EMPTY or DECL-LOOSE, and its first line reads "NOT APPLICABLE BY OPERATOR DECLARATION". DECL-LOOSE (HOLD) if declared gaps cover every consequential action type, or any action type with an effect record (C17) in the period. No later test may turn a HOLD from T1b into a PASS.
A gap is a missing sequence number (C3) or an uncounted drop (C14). Drops counted under a declared fail-open policy are reported, and do not fail.
Required fixtures: (i) empty-policy fixture (zero action types, no tolerances): expected HOLD, DECL-EMPTY. (ii) loose fixture (one action type, infinite gap tolerance): expected HOLD, DECL-LOOSE. (iii) minimal valid fixture: expected DECLARATION RECORDED.
Status: Spec.
Producer of the evidence: the operator (the declaration); the verifier prints it. The strength line does not judge whether the declaration is adequate (scope line 1 stands); it makes it visible.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A2. CLOCK GRADE (applies to every ordering claim; tests T6a to T6c and C19)
Scenario: Claims such as "record X was sealed before action Y took effect" rest on clocks. In the demonstration set-up the time sources sign the operator's own clock (T6c open), so ordering proves only that the operator's clock said so.
Test: For each ordering claim the verdict prints a grade:
  A: the seal time comes from an independent time-stamp authority or witness AND the effect time comes from a source independent of the operator (for example the receiving system's own record).
  B: the seal time comes from an independent time-stamp authority, but the effect time comes from the operator.
  C: the seal time comes from the operator's clock, whatever the source of the effect time.
Pass criterion: An ordering claim may PASS at any grade, but the grade is printed next to it, and a summary line counts claims at A, B and C. If any claim relies on a time source whose independence is not shown in the evidence, it is graded C. While T6c is open the verdict prints "T6c: OPEN (demo time sources sign the operator clock)".
Status: Spec (grading rule). T6c itself: open.
Producer of the evidence: independent time-stamp authority or witness (named by the operator in the declaration); effect time from the receiving system where one exists; otherwise no producer yet for grade A.
Public wording rule: do not describe any record in this release as "anchored" until T6c is closed. Use "time-stamped by [source], grade [A/B/C]". C19 is read as a time-source quorum; ordering is grade C until T6c closes. C19 itself is Spec at this release.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A3. SAMPLING POWER (applies to every sampled check)
Scenario: Where the verifier checks a random sample of s records out of n rather than all of them, a PASS says little unless the chance of catching bad records is stated.
Test: The verdict prints n (records in scope), s (records sampled, drawn without replacement using a seed the operator could not predict, see A4), the formula
  P(detect) = 1 - C(n-k, s) / C(n, s)
(the chance that at least one of k bad records is in the sample), and its value for k = 1, 10 and 100.
Pass criterion: The test passes if no sampled record fails. The printed chances are part of the result and cannot be omitted. The verdict also prints: "Sampling deters. Completeness rests on the deterministic checks (chain walk, counters), which check every record."
Worked figures (computed with Python's exact math.comb on 24 Oct 2026, checkable by anyone):
  n = 10,000, s = 100: k=1 0.0100; k=10 0.0957; k=100 0.6358
  n = 10,000, s = 300: k=1 0.0300; k=10 0.2627; k=100 0.9532
  n = 100,000, s = 1,000: k=1 0.0100; k=10 0.0956; k=100 0.6342
  n = 1,000,000, s = 1,000: k=1 0.0010; k=10 0.0100; k=100 0.0952
  To reach 95% at n = 10,000: k=100 needs s = 294; k=10 needs s = 2,588; k=1 needs s = 9,500.
Plain reading: for k = 1 the chance is exactly s/n. A sample catches a single altered record only rarely, which is why completeness must come from checks that walk every record.
Status: Spec.
Producer of the evidence: the verifier (sample selection and arithmetic); randomness source as in A4.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A4. T21c-GRIND (under C21)
Scenario: If the operator can make many exports of the same period and choose which one to show, or can see the public random round before fixing the export, it can grind until the sample misses its bad records.
Test: (i) Every export request is itself a logged event in the chain. (ii) The export is delivered to the verifier first. The verifier logs the receipt time and publishes the manifest hash. (iii) The sampling round is the first public randomness round (drand) after the verifier's receipt time, not chosen by the operator. (iv) The verdict prints the number of exports made for the period.
Pass criterion: PASS only if the verifier's published receipt time and manifest hash are earlier than the drand round used, the round equals the first round after that receipt time, and every export of the period known to the verifier appears as a logged event. If more than one export exists for the period, the verdict prints the count and lists them; the result is HOLD (reason EXPORT-MULTI) unless the profile allows multiple exports and all were committed before the round.
Fixtures: (i) single export committed before the round: PASS. (ii) round earlier than the verifier's receipt time: FAIL, GRIND-ORDER. (iii) an export known to the verifier from an independent source but absent from the chain: FAIL, EXPORT-UNLOGGED.
Status: Spec.
Producer of the evidence: operator (export events, manifest); the verifier (receipt time, published manifest hash); cosigners of the checkpoint (named in the declaration); time-stamp authority; drand (public randomness beacon). Vectors for this test will follow once the beacon round rules are confirmed against the drand documentation.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A5. T14e-SHADOW (under C14)
Scenario: A complete chain for declared channels says nothing about credentials, keys or endpoints the operator did not declare.
Test: Compare the declared set of credentials, keys and endpoints with an independent source that the operator does not write: whole-account billing records from the model provider, network egress logs held by another party, or the provider's key inventory. Any credential, key or endpoint seen in the independent source but not declared is reported by name.
Pass criterion: PASS only if an independent source is supplied and every item in it is declared. If an undeclared item is found: FAIL, SHADOW-FOUND, naming the item. If no independent source exists: the verdict prints "undeclared channels not testable" and the test result is HOLD (reason SHADOW-NO-SOURCE), never PASS.
Status: Spec.
Producer of the evidence: no producer yet. Candidates are the model provider (account-wide billing) or a network operator (egress logs). No producer has been arranged.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
A6. T4b-SPOOF (under C4; tool call and result pairing)
Scenario: A transcript shows a tool result for which no matching tool-call record exists (a result the model may have written itself), or a tool call with no matching result record. This is motivated by METR's investigation of the OpenAI and Hugging Face agent incident (26 August 2026), which reported intentionally spoofed tool calls in a share of reviewed transcripts and could not rule out edits.
Test: For each tool_result in the transcript, find the tool-call record with the same call identifier in the independent tool-side log; for each tool-call record, find its result. Check the channel label of each (who produced it).
Pass criterion: PASS only if every tool_result has exactly one matching tool-call record and every call has its result (or a logged failure). Otherwise FAIL with reason LABEL-MISMATCH, naming the record identifier and which side is missing. Fixtures: (i) result without call: FAIL, names the result. (ii) call without result or logged failure: FAIL, names the call. (iii) matched pair: PASS.
Limit: detects unpaired records only; spoofed content written inside the model's own text, with no record at all, is out of scope.
Placement: published under C4 (commitments to tool calls and results).
Status: Spec.
Producer of the evidence: the tool-side log (the tool host), which must be independent of the model's transcript; where the operator runs both, print "tool log not independent". A result that would have been PASS becomes HOLD (reason TOOL-LOG-NOT-INDEPENDENT); an unpaired record stays FAIL.

### V2.2 C7 Model identity (replaces the v1.1 C7 text)
The verifier SHALL report the highest C7 state (C7-0 to C7-5) that the record's evidence supports, the evidence used, and what evidence the next state would need. A record reaches state N only when every element required for state N verifies offline against public keys or published reference values identified in the record. The verifier SHALL also report the identifier precision of the model identity (alias, pinned version, or weights hash); a signature over an alias establishes the alias only. A record that names a model but carries only the operator's statement is reported as C7-0 (declared) and is not a failure of the record. Gap-table entries name evidence types, never suppliers.
C7-D Change visibility. Given two or more records for the same deployment, the verifier SHALL report whether a change of model identity between them is visible, at which identifier precision, and on whose assertion.
States: C7-0 Declared (someone said so). C7-1 Logged (operator's own tamper-evident log). C7-2 Relayed (an intermediary or third-party host signed what it requested and what came back). C7-3 Platform-asserted (the serving platform signed an invocation record naming the model; binds no output, identifies no weights). C7-4 Provider-signed (the model provider signed the output with model identity). C7-5 Hardware-attested (an attested enclave key signed the output and the measurement commits to a weights hash matching a published reference).
Qualifiers printed with every state: output-bound (yes/no); gap-visible (yes/no); freshness (challenge-bound, time-window, none); identifier precision (alias, pinned version, weights hash).
Caveat: C7 cannot establish which model weights produced an output without a provider signature over the output or an attestation rooted in hardware. States C7-0 to C7-3 establish who asserted the model identity, not what ran, and C7-3 does not bind any output. A signature by a hosting party that is not the model provider is graded C7-2 with that party named by role. C7-5 holds only within the hardware vendor's threat model, which excludes an attacker with physical access to the serving machine.
Test note: a verifier that omits identifier precision or C7-D from its output fails test T7-PREC.

### V2.3 Declared-gap reporting rule
A record set that declares a gap (a period, event type, channel or field it does not capture) is reported as DECLARED GAP for that scope, never as PASS for it. A gap the verifier detects that was not declared is reported as UNDECLARED GAP and is a FAIL. Every verdict lists both, together with the C22 item 6 coverage windows and the T14e-SHADOW result.

### V2.4 Unsigned results (printed on every result)
"Unsigned result. This is the output of the O4M Record Tests, published as plain text with the SHA-256 of the evidence it was run on. It is not a certificate, seal or approval. No certificates are issued."
Words not used in any result: certificate, certified, conformant, seal (to describe the result itself; C18 write-ahead seals are reported under their test name), attest (as a verb for the verifier), anchored (until T6c closes), compliant.

### V2.5 Model contract clause for buyers (informative, not legal advice)
Model identity evidence. For each output supplied under this agreement, the Supplier will keep, and give the Customer on request, evidence of which AI model and pinned model version produced it, at the highest level its serving arrangement allows: a provider or hardware-attested signature where available; otherwise a signed invocation record from the hosting platform; otherwise the Supplier's own tamper-evident log. The Supplier will state in writing which level applies and why a higher level is unavailable, and will give the Customer written notice before changing model, version, quantisation or host, and promptly once it becomes aware of any change made by its own provider behind the same model name. The Customer or its nominee must be able to check the evidence without the Supplier's systems.

## ANNEX A. LEGAL-EFFECT CROSSWALK (grade A sources only; not legal advice)
Row 1
Requirement: completeness and integrity tests (C1 to C23 as a whole; especially chain walk and counters)
Legal hook: Product Liability Directive, Directive (EU) 2024/2853, Article 9(1) (disclosure of evidence) and Article 10(2)(a) (defectiveness presumed where the defendant "fails to disclose relevant evidence pursuant to Article 9(1)"). Applies to products placed on the market or put into service after 9 December 2026 (Art. 2(1)).
Source opened: EUR-Lex HTML of OJ L 2024/2853, read 24 Oct 2026 (first 100,000 of 121,477 characters; Arts 2, 9, 10 within that range).
Grade: A for the wording of Arts 9(1), 10(2) and 2(1).
What a PASS supports: that the records an operator discloses can be checked by the other side for gaps and alteration within the declared scope, which bears on whether disclosure was of "relevant evidence" in usable form (Art. 9(6) lets courts require evidence "in an easily accessible and easily understandable manner").
What it does not support: it does not decide whether disclosure was adequate, whether the product was defective, or whether the presumption in Art. 10(2) applies; those are for the court. A PASS cannot rest on an empty or loose declaration (T1b); it still covers only the declared scope, so it cannot stand in for disclosure the declaration left out.


Row 2
Requirement: same as row 1, plus the excessive-difficulty route
Legal hook: PLD Article 10(4) (court shall presume defectiveness or causation where, despite disclosure, the claimant faces excessive difficulties due to technical or scientific complexity and shows likelihood).
Source opened: as row 1. Grade: A.
What a PASS supports: an outside check may reduce or show the technical difficulty of working with the disclosed records.
What it does not support: it says nothing about likelihood of defect or causation.


Row 3
Requirement: clock grade (A2), C19 time-source quorum, T21c-GRIND
Legal hook: eIDAS, Regulation (EU) No 910/2014, Article 41(1) (an electronic time stamp is not denied legal effect solely because it is electronic or not qualified) and Article 41(2) (a qualified electronic time stamp "shall enjoy the presumption of the accuracy of the date and the time it indicates and the integrity of the data to which the date and time are bound"); Article 42(1)(c) (signed or sealed by "the qualified trust service provider").
Source opened: EUR-Lex HTML of 32014R0910 (read from offset 100,000; Arts 41 and 42 read) and the consolidated version 02014R0910-20241018 (Art. 41(2) read again). Grade: A.
What a PASS supports: a grade A or B ordering claim that uses a qualified time stamp from a qualified trust service provider carries the Art. 41(2) presumption for the time stamp itself.
What it does not support: the presumption attaches only to a qualified time stamp from a qualified provider; demo time sources are not qualified, and nothing in this release carries the presumption. The presumption covers the time stamp's date and the data bound to it, not when the underlying action happened (that is why grade A also needs an independent effect time).


## ANNEX B. MUTATION-KILL MATRIX
State at release: no cell is filled from published code. In the RT-1 demonstrator (1 Nov 2026, self-reported), each of 14 checks was replaced in turn by one that always passes, and all 14 replacements were caught by at least one vector; finer mutants (off-by-one, swapped comparisons) have not been run. Every Demo row is "not yet checked" (NYC); every Spec row has no run. The matrix will be filled from recorded runs, each citing the script's SHA-256, before any vector is published. Known limits not killed by any test in this release: agreement between two implementations shows agreement only; a monitor's self-reported denominator cannot show its own silence; child processes spawned through a shell are invisible to declared channels; self-hosted open-weight runs lack an independent count.

## Known issues (open for comment until 1 Dec 2026)
- Profile W needs its own declaration set and its own T1b bounds; a SaaS customer cannot make every C1 declaration.
- Verifier shopping is not yet prevented. A public register of every result for a given evidence-bundle hash is under consideration.
- The result vocabulary will be closed to PASS, FAIL and HOLD, with annotations (DECLARED GAP, UNDECLARED GAP and the reason codes).
- "Gap" has two senses in this text (A1: a missing sequence number or uncounted drop; V2.3: a period, event type, channel or field not captured). They will be unified.
- The C19 row carries demonstration state (clock grade C) inside its requirement text; it will move to the status column.

Planned for the next version, after the comment window (no new C numbers; each strengthens an existing requirement):
- C22: a challenge-response liveness test. An outside monitor sends an unpredictable nonce, the capture point records it as a synthetic event, and the monitor checks it appears in the next checkpoint. A heartbeat shows a process is alive, not that capture works. Fault case: valid heartbeats while real events are dropped.
- Capture-layer statement: every verdict says which layers were present (application capture, host observation, execution witness, independent effect source), with a named threat boundary for root or hypervisor compromise, control-plane compromise, capture-daemon compromise, credential theft and network bypass.
- C20 and C23: independence defined by type (organisational, operational, implementation). The strongest result needs independently developed verifiers. Results are published at a location the verifier controls, so the result's origin can be checked even though it is unsigned.
- C2, C4, C6: one normative wire profile (one encoding per profile, duplicate-key rejection, number and null rules, schema version, hash domain separation; salt length, custody and opening protocol; Merkle tree construction, leaf order, empty and odd-node rules).
- C7: a separate inference-configuration identity (quantisation, adapters, tokenizer, parameters, routing and fallback), reported beside model identity.
- C17 and C18: a shared action identifier, with tests for duplicate or replayed actions and for a crash just before and just after an external effect.
- C14: request-ID scope (provider, tenant, endpoint) and treatment of retries, cancellations and partial streams.
- A registry of reason codes, with one meaning each.
- Status: Demo will require a published script, pinned revision, recorded environment, expected outputs, negative cases and a mutation-kill record.

## Related work on omission
Zhao et al., "Auditable Claims about AI Agents" (arXiv 2610.07459); Qin et al., "LLM Agents Can Easily Tamper With Their Own Traces" (arXiv 2609.30266); draft-sharif-agent-audit-trail (IETF individual draft). This release does not claim to be first.

FILE: check_exit_codes.py (SHA-256 fd3ba838126f9d0f393fdd71b80b436eeb3d3f26af8b920c42be1456a80b327b)
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# Copyright 2026 Gareth Wong
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#
# Checks the exit-3 paths of verify.py. These paths give no verdict, so they cannot be test vectors
# (a vector pins a verdict text). Run from the package root:
#   python check_exit_codes.py
# Exit code 0 if every case behaves as stated, otherwise 1. Prints one line per case.
#
# Not covered: an internal error that cannot be triggered from outside (verify.py catches every
# exception and exits 3). That path is reviewed by reading, not run.

import os
import subprocess
import sys

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
VERIFY = os.path.join(ROOT, "verify.py")
GOOD = os.path.join(ROOT, "vectors", "T3", "t3-001", "input.json")

# (name, arguments, expected exit code, must stdout hold a verdict line)
CASES = [
    ("control: valid input gives PASS", ["T3", GOOD], 0, True),
    ("missing file", ["T3", os.path.join(ROOT, "no-such-file.json")], 3, False),
    ("a folder instead of a file", ["T3", ROOT], 3, False),
    ("no arguments", [], 3, False),
    ("unknown test name", ["T9", GOOD], 3, False),
    ("alternative reading given for T3", ["T3", GOOD, "--reading", "B"], 3, False),
    ("unknown reading letter", ["T14a", GOOD, "--reading", "Z"], 3, False),
]


def main():
    bad = 0
    for name, args, want, verdict in CASES:
        p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-I", VERIFY] + args, capture_output=True)
        out = p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace")
        has_verdict = any(l.startswith("Result: ") for l in out.splitlines())
        ok = p.returncode == want and has_verdict == verdict
        if not ok:
            bad += 1
        print("%s | %s | exit %d (want %d), verdict line %s (want %s)"
              % ("OK  " if ok else "FAIL", name, p.returncode, want,
                 "present" if has_verdict else "absent", "present" if verdict else "absent"))
    print("%d cases, %d failed" % (len(CASES), bad))
    return 1 if bad else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
END OF FILE: check_exit_codes.py

FILE: gen_vectors.py (SHA-256 6b2f60e0c40bdc7e7fcc4a11f5db0590ef02363b8be3649d4793858ecb96e96e)
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# Copyright 2026 Gareth Wong
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#
# Deterministic generator for the T3 and T14a vectors (vector profile v0).
# No randomness, no network, no clock. Running it twice gives identical bytes.
#
#   python gen_vectors.py OUTDIR              writes input.json, about.txt, intent.txt
#   python gen_vectors.py OUTDIR --expected   also runs verify.py and writes expected.txt and
#                                             expected_exit.txt, and stops if any result or
#                                             reason-code set differs from the stated intent.

import hashlib
import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys

ZERO = "0" * 64


def canonical(obj):
    return json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8")


def rehash(r):
    body = {k: v for k, v in r.items() if k != "hash"}
    r["hash"] = hashlib.sha256(canonical(body)).hexdigest()
    return r


def rec(sid, seq, prev, data, kind="event", last_seq=None):
    r = {"stream_id": sid, "seq": seq, "prev_hash": prev, "type": kind, "data": data}
    if kind == "teardown":
        r["last_seq"] = last_seq
    return rehash(r)


def stream(sid, n=5, start=1, prefix=None):
    prefix = prefix or sid
    out, prev = [], ZERO
    for i in range(n):
        r = rec(sid, start + i, prev, "%s event %d" % (prefix, start + i))
        out.append(r)
        prev = r["hash"]
    last = start + n - 1
    out.append(rec(sid, last + 1, prev, "%s teardown" % prefix, "teardown", last))
    return out


def relink(rs, from_index):
    # recompute prev_hash and hash from from_index onwards, in list order
    for i in range(from_index, len(rs)):
        rs[i]["prev_hash"] = rs[i - 1]["hash"] if i > 0 else ZERO
        rehash(rs[i])
    return rs


def decl(fail_mode="fail-closed", gaps=None):
    return {
        "consequential_action_types": ["payment"],
        "tolerances": {"max_gap": 0, "max_clock_skew_s": 5, "max_unsealed_window_s": 60},
        "fail_mode": fail_mode,
        "monitors": [],
        "declared_gaps": gaps or [],
    }


def cp(cid, r, a, d, policy="fail-closed", **extra):
    c = {"checkpoint_id": cid, "received": r, "accepted": a, "dropped": d, "policy": policy}
    c.update(extra)
    return c


V = []  # (folder, test, reading, input object or raw text, result, codes, about)


def add(folder, test, obj, result, codes, about, reading=None):
    V.append((folder, test, reading, obj, result, sorted(codes), about))


# T3 vectors

s = stream("s1")
add("T3/t3-001", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "PASS", [],
    "Valid single stream: five events (seq 1 to 5) and a teardown at seq 6 carrying last_seq 5, every hash and link "
    "recomputed. Models the untampered case. Expected PASS with no reason codes.")

a, b = stream("s1"), stream("s2")
inter = [x for pair in zip(a, b) for x in pair]
add("T3/t3-002", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": inter}, "PASS", [],
    "Two valid streams whose records alternate in file order. Models normal interleaved capture. The verifier walks "
    "each stream by seq, so interleaving does not matter. Expected PASS.")

s = stream("s1"); del s[2]
add("T3/t3-003", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-GAP", "T3-LINK"],
    "Deletion attack: the event at seq 3 is removed and nothing is recomputed. Seq 3 is missing (T3-GAP, also listed "
    "as an UNDECLARED GAP) and seq 4 still points at the deleted record, so it does not link to seq 2 (T3-LINK). Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); del s[0]
add("T3/t3-004", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-GAP", "T3-START"],
    "Deletion of the first event. The lowest seq is now 2 (T3-START) and seq 1 is missing (T3-GAP, listed as an "
    "UNDECLARED GAP). No link failure is reported for seq 2 because it has no predecessor left to compare with; T3-START "
    "carries that finding. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); del s[4]
add("T3/t3-005", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-GAP", "T3-LINK", "T3-TAIL"],
    "Deletion of the last event before the teardown (seq 5). Three codes fire: T3-GAP (seq 5 missing), T3-LINK (the "
    "teardown at seq 6 points at the deleted record, not at seq 4) and T3-TAIL (the teardown says last_seq 5 but the "
    "highest event present is 4). The teardown carrying last_seq is what makes this tail deletion visible. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1")[:-1]
add("T3/t3-006", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "HOLD", ["T3-OPEN"],
    "The teardown is removed, leaving an open stream of five valid events. T3 cannot tell whether events after seq 5 "
    "existed, so it returns HOLD with T3-OPEN rather than PASS. Known limit: deleting the tail of a stream that has no "
    "teardown cannot be seen by T3 alone; it needs C13 (teardown on a never-shed lane) or counters and checkpoints (C14, C6). Expected HOLD.")

s = stream("s1"); s[2]["seq"], s[3]["seq"] = 4, 3
add("T3/t3-007", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-HASH", "T3-LINK"],
    "Reorder attack: the seq values of events 3 and 4 are swapped without recomputing anything. Both moved records "
    "fail hash recomputation (T3-HASH, seq is inside the hash) and the links at seq 3, 4 and 5 no longer match their "
    "predecessors (T3-LINK). Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); s.insert(3, rec("s1", 3, s[1]["hash"], "s1 inserted record"))
add("T3/t3-008", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-FORK"],
    "Insertion attack: an extra record reuses seq 3 with different data, a valid hash and a valid link to seq 2. Two "
    "records now share seq 3 (T3-FORK). Seq 4 still links to one of them, so no link failure fires. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1")
alt4 = rec("s1", 4, s[2]["hash"], "s1 branch event 4")
alt5 = rec("s1", 5, alt4["hash"], "s1 branch event 5")
alt6 = rec("s1", 6, alt5["hash"], "s1 branch teardown", "teardown", 5)
add("T3/t3-009", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s + [alt4, alt5, alt6]}, "FAIL", ["T3-FORK", "T3-TAIL"],
    "Fork: a second branch starts at seq 4 linking to seq 3 and runs to its own teardown, so seq 4, 5 and 6 each hold "
    "two records (T3-FORK) and the stream has two teardowns (T3-TAIL). Each branch is internally valid, which is why "
    "only the fork and tail checks catch it. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); s[2]["data"] = "s1 event 3 altered"
add("T3/t3-010", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-HASH"],
    "Tampering with the data of event 3 while leaving its stored hash unchanged. Recomputation does not match "
    "(T3-HASH). Links still match the stored hashes, so only T3-HASH fires. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); s[2]["data"] = "s1 event 3 altered"; rehash(s[2])
add("T3/t3-011", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-LINK"],
    "Tampering with event 3 and recomputing that record's hash, but not the records after it. Event 3 now verifies on "
    "its own, but seq 4 points at the old hash (T3-LINK). Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); s[2]["data"] = "s1 event 3 altered"; relink(s, 2)
add("T3/t3-012", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "PASS", [],
    "Known limit vector. Event 3 is altered and every record from seq 3 onwards is recomputed consistently, including "
    "the teardown. The chain walk returns PASS because the rewritten chain is internally perfect. A chain walk alone "
    "cannot detect a full rewrite from the point of tampering; that needs external commitments made before the rewrite "
    "(checkpoints with independent time-stamps and witnesses, C6 and T6a). This vector must stay in the package. Expected PASS.")

add("T3/t3-013", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": []}, "HOLD", ["T3-EMPTY"],
    "Zero streams: the records list is empty. Nothing can be walked, so the result is HOLD with T3-EMPTY, never PASS. Expected HOLD.")

s = stream("s1", n=5, start=0)
add("T3/t3-014", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s}, "FAIL", ["T3-START"],
    "Zero-based stream: seq runs 0 to 4 and the teardown is at 5, all hashes and links valid. Vector profile v0 chose "
    "one-based numbering, so this fails T3-START. It documents the choice; if the editor picks zero-based numbering, "
    "this vector and t3-001 swap results. Expected FAIL.")

s = stream("s1"); order = [3, 0, 5, 1, 4, 2]

    "The records of t3-001 in a fixed shuffled file order (4, 1, teardown, 2, 5, 3). Content is valid; the verifier "
    "walks by seq, so file order does not matter. Expected PASS.")

a, b, c = stream("s1"), stream("s2"), stream("s3"); b[1]["data"] = "s2 event 2 altered"

    "Three streams; stream s2 has event 2 altered with its stored hash unchanged. Only s2 is named in the detail "

s = stream("s1"); s[5]["last_seq"] = 7; rehash(s[5])

def deep(k):
    x = []
    for _ in range(k):
        x = [x]
    return x


s = stream("s1")
add("T3/t3-029", "T3", {"declaration": decl(), "records": s + [deep(14)]}, "HOLD", ["INPUT-MALFORMED"],
    "Added 10 Nov 2026 (package 2026-11-10). A valid stream plus one element nested 15 arrays deep, so the file nests 17 "
    "levels (limit 16). The depth scan rejects it before parsing: INPUT-MALFORMED, HOLD, 'nesting deeper than 16'. Models "
    "a file shaped to crash or confuse a parser. The verdict does not depend on the Python version. Expected HOLD.")

s = stream("s1")

    "Added 10 Nov 2026 (package 2026-11-10). The same valid input as t3-001 saved with a UTF-8 byte order mark at the start, "
    "as some Windows editors do. Vector profile v0 does not strip it (AMBIGUITY_LOG entry 35): INPUT-UNPARSEABLE, HOLD. "
    "Expected HOLD.")

s = stream("s1")

big = 2 ** 53
add("T14a/t14a-014", "T14a", {"declaration": decl(), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", big, big, 0)]}, "HOLD", ["T14A-MALFORMED"],
    "Counters of 2^53 (9007199254740992), one above the largest integer a JSON reader using IEEE 754 doubles holds "
    "exactly. Profile v0 bounds integers at 2^53 minus 1, so this is malformed. A reader in another language could "
    "silently round these numbers and reach a different result. Expected HOLD.")

add("T14a/t14a-015", "T14a", {"declaration": decl(), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", 100, 100, 0), cp("cp-1", 200, 200, 0)]},
    "HOLD", ["T14A-MALFORMED"],
    "Two checkpoints share the id cp-1. The second is malformed (T14A-MALFORMED) and set aside; the first is checked "
    "and balances. Expected HOLD.")

add("T14a/t14a-016", "T14a", {"declaration": decl("fail-closed"), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", 100, 98, 2, "fail-open")]},
    "FAIL", ["T14A-POLICY"],
    "The declaration says fail-closed but the checkpoint says fail-open, and two records were dropped. C1 lists a "
    "retroactive policy change as an attack case. Proposed code T14A-POLICY, FAIL. Expected FAIL.")

add("T14a/t14a-017", "T14a", {"declaration": decl(), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", 100, 100, 0, provenance="UNVERIFIED")]},
    "HOLD", ["T14A-UNVERIFIED"],
    "Balanced counters labelled UNVERIFIED (for example a copy of an outside count delivered inside the operator's own "
    "export). Spec Conventions: a check resting on an UNVERIFIED source can return FAIL but never PASS. HOLD with "
    "proposed code T14A-UNVERIFIED. Expected HOLD.")

add("T14a/t14a-018", "T14a",
    {"declaration": decl(), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", 100, 100, 0, provenance="INDEPENDENTLY-RECONCILED")]}, "PASS", [],
    "The operator's own input labels its counters INDEPENDENTLY-RECONCILED. That label is the operator's assertion; "
    "T14a has no reconciliation evidence, so it prints the label as claimed and not checked, and the PASS still carries "
    "the SOURCE-LOCAL line. Expected PASS.")

add("T14a/t14a-019", "T14a", {"declaration": decl(), "checkpoints": [cp("cp-1", 100, 99, 0)]}, "FAIL", ["T14A-IMBALANCE"],
    "Added after the mutation run (mutant M-T14-06, tolerate an imbalance of 1, survived without it). Received 100, "
    "accepted 99, dropped 0: one record lost without being counted. The declared maximum gap is 0, so a loss of one "
    "is a FAIL. Expected FAIL.")

# Alternative readings (not the primary reading)

inc = [cp("cp-1", 100, 100, 0), cp("cp-2", 200, 199, 1), cp("cp-3", 300, 298, 2)]

    "level. Expected PASS under Reading B.", reading="B")

    "Reading B. Checkpoint cp-3 reports lower received, accepted and dropped counts than cp-2 while each checkpoint "
    "balances on its own. A counter reset like this hides what happened in between. Under the primary reading this "
    "vector passes. Expected FAIL with proposed T14A-REGRESS under Reading B.", reading="B")

    "Reading C (accepted equals the number of records in the chain up to the checkpoint). The chain holds 6 records "

    "under Reading C.", reading="C")

    "on their own, so the primary reading passes; Reading C ties them to the chain and fails with proposed T14A-CHAIN.",
    reading="C")

    "under fail-open do not fail (A1) but the scope they cover is not PASS either, so the result is HOLD with "
    "DECLARED-GAP. Expected HOLD under Reading D.", reading="D")


def write(path, data):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(data.encode("utf-8"))


def main(argv):
    if len(argv) not in (2, 3) or (len(argv) == 3 and argv[2] != "--expected"):
        sys.stderr.write("usage: gen_vectors.py OUTDIR [--expected]\n")
        return 3
    out = argv[1]
    if out.startswith("-"):
        # 7 Nov 2026 bug: "gen_vectors.py --expected" took "--expected" as the output folder.
        sys.stderr.write("usage: gen_vectors.py OUTDIR [--expected]   (OUTDIR must not start with '-')\n")
        return 3
    here = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    bad = 0
    for folder, test, reading, obj, result, codes, about in V:
        d = os.path.join(out, "vectors", folder)
        text = obj if isinstance(obj, str) else json.dumps(obj, indent=2, ensure_ascii=True) + "\n"
        write(os.path.join(d, "input.json"), text)
        write(os.path.join(d, "about.txt"), about + "\n")
        cmd = "python verify.py %s vectors/%s/input.json%s" % (test, folder, (" --reading " + reading) if reading else "")
        write(os.path.join(d, "intent.txt"),
              "command: %s\nintended result: %s\nintended reason codes: %s\n"
              % (cmd, result, ", ".join(codes) if codes else "none"))
        if len(argv) == 3:
            args = [sys.executable, "-I", os.path.join(here, "verify.py"), test, os.path.join(d, "input.json")]
            if reading:
                args += ["--reading", reading]
            p = subprocess.run(args, capture_output=True)
            stdout = p.stdout.decode("utf-8")
            write(os.path.join(d, "expected.txt"), stdout)
            write(os.path.join(d, "expected_exit.txt"), "%d\n" % p.returncode)
            got_res = [l for l in stdout.splitlines() if l.startswith("Result: ")][0][8:]
            got_codes = [l for l in stdout.splitlines() if l.startswith("Reason codes")][0].split(": ", 1)[1]
            got_codes = [] if got_codes == "none" else got_codes.split(", ")
            if got_res != result or got_codes != codes:
                bad += 1
                sys.stderr.write("MISMATCH %s: intended %s %s, verifier gave %s %s\n" % (folder, result, codes, got_res, got_codes))
    sys.stderr.write("%d vectors written, %d mismatches against intent\n" % (len(V), bad))
    return 1 if bad else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))
END OF FILE: gen_vectors.py

On survivors and history
First run on 7 Nov 2026: 47 mutants, 44 killed, 3 survived (M-T3-04 skip the genesis check, M-T3-06, M-T14-06 tolerate an imbalance of 1). Two survivors showed missing vectors, not equivalent code. Vectors t3-028 (valid first record with a non-zero prev_hash) and t14a-019 (imbalance of exactly 1) were added, every expected.txt was regenerated from the unchanged verifier, and both mutants are now killed. Mutants M-T3-06b, M-T3-06c and M-T3-20 were added at the same time. No expected.txt was edited to kill a mutant.
M-T3-06 survives and is an equivalent mutant: the highest seq in a stream is present by construction (it is taken from the records), so leaving it out of the missing-seq scan cannot change any output. No vector can kill it. The sharper off-by-one mutants M-T3-06b and M-T3-06c are killed.
Limits of this record: mutants are hand-written text substitutions chosen by the same author who wrote the verifier, so they reflect that author's idea of likely mistakes. A kill shows that a vector disagrees with the mutant; it does not show that the expected outputs are right. Agreement with RT-1 or with a second reader has not been checked.

FILE: mutation/mutate.py (SHA-256 859b9e86e56398e82aa590de9a102380de4911f29bdc67af4582c0fbaa84e69a)
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# Copyright 2026 Gareth Wong
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#
# Mutation-kill harness for verify.py. Each mutant is one exact text substitution in a copy of
# verify.py. Every vector (including alternative readings) is run against the mutant; a vector
# kills the mutant if its stdout or exit code differs from expected.txt or expected_exit.txt.
# expected.txt files are never edited here.
#
#   python mutation/mutate.py            (run from the package root)
# Writes mutation/MUTATION_RECORD.txt. Exit code 0 if the unmutated verifier matches every
# vector, otherwise 1.

import datetime
import hashlib
import os
import platform
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

# (id, description, old text, new text). old text must occur exactly once in verify.py.
MUTANTS = [
    ("M-T3-01", "T3 always PASS (all T3 checks skipped)",
     "def run_t3(top, decl, v):\n", "def run_t3(top, decl, v):\n    return\n"),
    ("M-T3-02", "T3 always FAIL",
     "def run_t3(top, decl, v):\n", "def run_t3(top, decl, v):\n    v.add(\"T3-HASH\", \"mutant\")\n    return\n"),
    ("M-T3-03", "skip the hash recomputation",
     'if record_hash(r) != r["hash"]:', "if False:"),
    ("M-T3-04", "skip the genesis (zero prev_hash) check",
     'if r["prev_hash"] != ZERO_HASH:', "if False:"),
    ("M-T3-05", "accept first seq 0",
     "if lowest != 1:", "if lowest not in (0, 1):"),
    ("M-T3-06", "off-by-one in the gap test: highest seq left out of the scan (equivalent mutant, see note)",
     "range(1, highest + 1)", "range(1, highest)"),
    ("M-T3-06b", "off-by-one in the gap test: seq 1 left out of the scan",
     "range(1, highest + 1)", "range(2, highest + 1)"),
    ("M-T3-06c", "off-by-one in the gap test: scan runs one past the highest seq",
     "range(1, highest + 1)", "range(1, highest + 2)"),
    ("M-T3-20", "link compared only with seq minus one (a missing predecessor is never a link failure)",
     "pred = seqs[idx - 1]", "pred = s - 1\n        if pred not in by_seq:\n            continue"),
    ("M-T3-07", "skip the link check",
     'if r["prev_hash"] not in pred_hashes:', "if False:"),

     "if len(by_seq[s]) > 1:", "if False:"),

     'elif td["last_seq"] != max_event or td["seq"] != td["last_seq"] + 1:', "elif False:"),

     'v.add("T3-OPEN"', 'None and v.add("T3-OPEN"'),

     "codes = sorted({c for c, _ in v.findings})", "codes = sorted({c for c, _ in v.findings})[:1]"),

     "seqs = sorted(by_seq)", "seqs = list(by_seq)"),
    ("M-T3-13", "accept more than one teardown",
     "if len(tds) > 1:", "if False:"),
    ("M-T3-14", "accept records after the teardown",
     'if td["seq"] != highest or any(e["seq"] > td["seq"] for e in events):', "if False:"),
    ("M-T3-15", "treat an empty input as PASS",
     'v.add("T3-EMPTY"', 'None and v.add("T3-EMPTY"'),
    ("M-T3-16", "accept uppercase hex",
     'HEX = set("0123456789abcdef")', 'HEX = set("0123456789abcdefABCDEF")'),
    ("M-T3-17", "declared-gap range ignored (declared seqs still counted as undeclared)",
     "und = [s for s in missing if s not in declared_seqs]", "und = missing"),
    ("M-T3-18", "declared gap reported without the DECLARED-GAP hold (declared scope can PASS)",
     'v.add("DECLARED-GAP", "stream', 'None and v.add("DECLARED-GAP", "stream'),
    ("M-T3-19", "missing seqs not listed as UNDECLARED GAP",
     'v.undeclared.append("stream', 'None and v.undeclared.append("stream'),
    ("M-IN-01", "booleans accepted as integers",
     "return type(v) is int and", "return isinstance(v, int) and"),
    ("M-IN-02", "duplicate keys accepted (last one wins)",

def sha(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        return hashlib.sha256(f.read()).hexdigest()


def vectors():
    out = []
    base = os.path.join(ROOT, "vectors")
    for group in sorted(os.listdir(base)):
        for vid in sorted(os.listdir(os.path.join(base, group))):
            d = os.path.join(base, group, vid)
            with open(os.path.join(d, "intent.txt"), encoding="utf-8") as f:
                cmd = f.readline().split(": ", 1)[1].split()
            # cmd: python verify.py TEST path [--reading X]
            out.append((vid, cmd[2], os.path.join(ROOT, cmd[3]), cmd[4:], d))
    return out


def run_all(verifier, vecs):
    disagree = []
    for vid, test, path, extra, d in vecs:
        p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-I", verifier, test, path] + extra, capture_output=True)
        with open(os.path.join(d, "expected.txt"), "rb") as f:
            exp = f.read()
        with open(os.path.join(d, "expected_exit.txt"), encoding="utf-8") as f:
            exp_exit = int(f.read().strip())
        if p.stdout != exp or p.returncode != exp_exit:
            disagree.append(vid)
    return disagree


def main():
    src_path = os.path.join(ROOT, "verify.py")
    with open(src_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        src = f.read()
    vecs = vectors()
    started = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S UTC")
    base_disagree = run_all(src_path, vecs)
    lines = []
    killed = survived = 0
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        for mid, desc, old, new in MUTANTS:
            n = src.count(old)
            if n != 1:
                lines.append("%s | %s | NOT APPLIED (target text found %d times)" % (mid, desc, n))
                continue
            mpath = os.path.join(tmp, "verify_%s.py" % mid.replace("-", "_"))
            with open(mpath, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
                f.write(src.replace(old, new))
            dis = run_all(mpath, vecs)
            if dis:
                killed += 1
                lines.append("%s | %s | killed by %s" % (mid, desc, ", ".join(dis)))
            else:
                survived += 1
                lines.append("%s | %s | SURVIVED" % (mid, desc))
    me = os.path.abspath(__file__)
    rec = [
        "MUTATION RECORD, O4M Record Tests 2026 v1.0, T3 and T14a, vector profile v0",
        "Self-run by the author of the verifier. Not reproduced by anyone else. Finer mutants beyond this list have not been run.",
        "",
        "Run started: %s" % started,
        "Python: %s (%s)" % (platform.python_version(), platform.platform(terse=True)),
        "SHA-256 verify.py: %s" % sha(src_path),
        "SHA-256 gen_vectors.py: %s" % sha(os.path.join(ROOT, "gen_vectors.py")),
        "SHA-256 mutation/mutate.py: %s" % sha(me),
        "Commands (from the package root):",
        "  python gen_vectors.py . --expected",
        "  python mutation/mutate.py",
        "Vectors run per mutant: %d (T3, T14a and alternative readings; a vector kills a mutant if stdout or exit code differs from expected)" % len(vecs),
        "Unmutated verifier against every vector: %s" % ("all match" if not base_disagree else "MISMATCH: " + ", ".join(base_disagree)),
        "",
        "One line per mutant: id | description | result",
    ] + lines + [
        "",
        "Mutants: %d. Killed: %d. Survived: %d." % (len(MUTANTS), killed, survived),
        "",
        "On survivors and history",
        "First run on 7 Nov 2026: 47 mutants, 44 killed, 3 survived (M-T3-04 skip the genesis check, M-T3-06, "
        "M-T14-06 tolerate an imbalance of 1). Two survivors showed missing vectors, not equivalent code. Vectors "
        "t3-028 (valid first record with a non-zero prev_hash) and t14a-019 (imbalance of exactly 1) were added, "
        "every expected.txt was regenerated from the unchanged verifier, and both mutants are now killed. Mutants "
        "M-T3-06b, M-T3-06c and M-T3-20 were added at the same time. No expected.txt was edited to kill a mutant.",
        "M-T3-06 survives and is an equivalent mutant: the highest seq in a stream is present by construction (it is "
        "taken from the records), so leaving it out of the missing-seq scan cannot change any output. No vector can "
        "kill it. The sharper off-by-one mutants M-T3-06b and M-T3-06c are killed.",
        "Limits of this record: mutants are hand-written text substitutions chosen by the same author who wrote the "
        "verifier, so they reflect that author's idea of likely mistakes. A kill shows that a vector disagrees with the "
        "mutant; it does not show that the expected outputs are right. Agreement with RT-1 or with a second reader has "
        "not been checked.",
    ]
    with open(os.path.join(ROOT, "mutation", "MUTATION_RECORD.txt"), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
        f.write("\n".join(rec) + "\n")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(lines[-0:]) + "\nkilled %d survived %d base %s\n" % (killed, survived, base_disagree or "ok"))
    return 0 if not base_disagree else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
END OF FILE: mutation/mutate.py

FILE: vectors/T3/t3-012/about.txt (SHA-256 9d1812a67c812f2eac1271bf65cb1c8fa853f6689cba351a9b1ae7f4fddf253d)
Known limit vector. Event 3 is altered and every record from seq 3 onwards is recomputed consistently, including the teardown. The chain walk returns PASS because the rewritten chain is internally perfect. A chain walk alone cannot detect a full rewrite from the point of tampering; that needs external commitments made before the rewrite (checkpoints with independent time-stamps and witnesses, C6 and T6a). This vector must stay in the package. Expected PASS.
